#2184. Bournemouth blocked Birmingham City and Southampton deals - the tables have turned

Birmingham City and Southampton have emerged as two of the early-season promotion candidates in the Championship, with the goals certainly flowing at St. Andrew's @ Knighthead Park and St Mary's respectively.

Chris Davies has addressed Birmingham's shortcomings from last season rather emphatically, whilst Saints haven't let their promotion ambitions be deterred by a four-point deduction they were served by the EFL.

Both managers have quality running right throughout their squads, and they have been particularly efficient in attack, with their sides scoring over 50 goals between them.

In the summer, the West Midlands and South Coast outfits were just two of the clubs heavily linked with a move for AFC Bournemouth attacker, Daniel Jebbison.

Jebbison is no stranger to the Championship, best-known for his time at Sheffield United, before moving to the Vitality Stadium.

However, based off early-season fortunes for the relevant parties, many will argue that Marco Rose has made the wrong call surrounding the 23-year-old's career.

The nine-time Canadian international's prior exploits have certainly garnered intrigue, as despite only scoring three times in 35 appearances at Bramall Lane, Jebbison sealed a £1.5m move to the Dorset club under Andoni Iraola.

Utilised as a bit-part player in 2024/25, the forward still made 34 appearances in all competitions, albeit many of those were as a substitute, before a loan move was sanctioned to Preston North End last summer.

Last season was one of extremely mixed fortunes for the Lilywhites and Jebbison, with the Oakville native netting six times in 38 appearances - the majority of which came in the first half of the campaign, before the form of Paul Heckingbottom's side dropped like a stone.

Football League World ranks all 24 Championship clubs by the longest-serving member of their squad.

Despite seeing Rose replace Iraola on the South Coast, it was largely expected that Jebbison would be allowed to leave on loan or permanently in the summer window, and plenty of Championship sides were linked.

Two of those came in the form of Birmingham and Saints, with Davies looking to reshuffle his attacking options following Kyogo Furuhashi's failed transfer to St. Andrew's, whilst Eckert searched for even more firepower beyond Cyle Larin.

Blackburn Rovers and Wrexham were also credited with interest in the striker, before it emerged three days prior to the Championship season kick-off that Enas Unal's sale from Bournemouth to Getafe presented Jebbison with the chance to stake a claim for regular first-team minutes.

It has been an intriguing start to life in English football for Rose, with Bournemouth recording mixed results in the Premier League, although they have made a flying start to their maiden UEFA Europa League campaign.

There were reservations over how the German's squad would cope with the swift turnarounds that come with such short-term success, and Jebbison, who also spent time on loan with Watford, claimed in September that he would be ready whenever needed.

"I'll get my chance and every minute I get, every second or whatever, I'll work my hardest so that's what I'm trying to do, just take it day-by-day," he said.

However, that is yet to be the case, as he still finds himself behind Evanilson, Alvaro Rodriguez and Junior Kroupi in the pecking order, which could see him potentially have one eye on a January exit.

Whilst there were some concerns over the summer transfer policies at Birmingham and Southampton, it's fair to say neither club has been impacted negatively by such a domino effect.

Davies' tactical reshuffles have allowed his creative players to flourish, with January signing, August Priske, spearheading the attack in fine fashion, whilst Jay Stansfield is certainly now more settled in his 'number 10' role.

Blues and Saints fans, would Daniel Jebbison have made any difference to your clubs' impressive form? Comment below!

Saints have been without Larin's prolificacy in recent matches due to a hamstring issue, but Jebbison's compatriot's stellar record of 15 goals in his first 27 Championship games means Eckert's side will always be in with a shout of continuing their strong form when the powerful striker is back available.

#2183. Three goals from 1.09 xG: £20m star’s stunning form inspires Birmingham City’s rise to the top of the Championship

Birmingham City defeated fellow promotion hopefuls Swansea City on Saturday, making it six wins from the last seven to move to 1st in the Championship. BCFC have put in some fantastic team performances in this run, but as far as individual efforts go, it is hard to overlook that of Jay Stansfield.

The £20m Blues man has moved back to striker in August Priske‘s absence for the last two games, and he has netted three goals. In victories over Millwall and the Swans, Stansfield has over-performed his xG of 1.09 by 1.91 (FotMob), providing a timely reminder of his goalscoring threat at St. Andrew’s.

Playing as an attacking midfielder, Stansfield has been more of a facilitator than a goalscorer in recent times, with his well-rounded game and fantastic work rate seeing him retain a key role in Chris Davies’ side. Blues fans needed no reminder of his importance, but the 24-year-old’s return to the scoresheet in the last two games has affirmed just how good he is as the lead man up top.

Stansfield is one of the faces of Birmingham City’s rebuild. He impressed on loan despite relegation in the 2023/24 season, and was the statement signing of Tom Wagner and co ahead of their record-breaking League One title win, joining permanently for a reported fee of up to £20m.

23 goals in 44 games across the 2024/25 campaign helped inspire City back to the Championship, and he followed that up with 11 goals in all competitions last term. However, a move out of his natural striker role has seen goals decrease for Stansfield.

Before November started, Stansfield had just three league goals in 2026. As said before, his work rate in and out of possession and his technical ability in knitting attacks together meant he has held an influential role regardless – for the most part. However, three goals in four days have made the Tiverton-born forward pivotal in BCFC’s rise to the Championship summit.

Stansfield was everywhere against Swansea City. Not only did he score twice, but he created two chances and one big chance, took seven shots and covered plenty of ground (FotMob). His heatmap showed that, despite playing as the traditional striker, he was still dropping deep and drifting wide to pick up the ball and keep the Swans’ defence thinking.

It was the sort of performance that everyone knows Stansfield is capable of. He led by example in terms of both attribution and ability as Davies’ side moved top.

Birmingham City’s faith in Davies has paid off. After doubts over whether he could lead them to the next level, he has weathered the storm and turned his side into deserving Championship leaders.

The Welshman has a tough call to make amid Stansfield’s return to goalscoring form though. At striker, it feels like his entire skillset is being used as he finds more shooting opportunities.

Stansfield is more of a scoring threat at striker compared to attacking midfield. However, the form of Danish ace Priske at the top of the pitch cannot be ignored, and he is not capable of dropping into a deeper role like his attacking counterpart.

It is highly likely that Stansfield drops back into the no. 10 role when Priske is fit again. But, recent performances and historic stats suggest that Blues get their best out of their big-money star when he’s deployed at the top of the pitch.

It is a welcome dilemma for Davies to have, as Birmingham City enter the break on a high.

#2182. ‘Embarrassing themselves!’ Jamie Carragher hits back at Rio Ferdinand over Cristiano Ronaldo defence

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Jamie Carragher has taken aim at Rio Ferdinand for defending Cristiano Ronaldo following the Portuguese forward’s latest controversy with his national team.

Ronaldo has found himself at the centre of a growing dispute after abruptly leaving Portugal’s national team camp following a reported disagreement with head coach Jorge Jesus. The incident has prompted strong reactions from former teammates and pundits, with Ferdinand firmly siding with the Al-Nassr star.

Jamie Carragher, however, believes Ronaldo’s reputation should not shield him from criticism when his behaviour becomes difficult to defend.

Rio Ferdinand, who played alongside Cristiano Ronaldo at Manchester United between 2003 and 2009, criticised Jorge Jesus for the way he handled the situation.

Speaking on his Rio Presents podcast, the former United captain criticised Jesus for not showing respect to Cristiano Ronaldo.

“Cristiano Ronaldo was put in a very bad situation and was treated in an inappropriate way. He has an enormous history in football. Jorge Jesus did not deal with Ronaldo well and chose to treat him in that way, but I cannot find any explanation for it,” Ferdinand said.

Despite Jesus being Portugal’s manager, Ferdinand questioned his standing, comparing him with the likes of Sir Alex Ferguson and Pep Guardiola. To say the least, Ferdinand only embarrassed himself doing that.

Jamie Carragher saw the argument very differently and rightfully so.

Speaking on The Overlap Daily, the former Liverpool defender suggested that Ferdinand’s friendship with Ronaldo was influencing his reaction to the incident.

“People think it’s good to be close to people in the game, but this is where it becomes a problem,” Carragher explained.

He then called out both Ferdinand and broadcaster Piers Morgan, who has also been vocal in his support of Ronaldo.

“People like Piers Morgan and Rio Ferdinand, who are big people in the media whether you like them or not, are embarrassing themselves with the nonsense that they’re coming out with because they know Ronaldo. You can know someone and try to look after them, but you cannot defend the indefensible.”

Carragher believes Ronaldo’s achievements should not put the forward beyond criticism, particularly when his actions involve his national team and its management.

Gary Neville, who also played with Cristiano Ronaldo, has questioned Ronaldo’s handling of the situation, despite his longstanding affection for his former Manchester United teammate.

Neville argued that Ronaldo’s public response had made matters worse and the forward needed to accept criticism rather than expect others to defend him.

“With Cristiano, it’s difficult because I love him, but I can’t watch what happened at United in his second period and then see him make a statement like that without saying something. He has to be pulled up on that and told that it isn’t acceptable,” Neville said.

He also suggested Ronaldo’s extraordinary success, wealth and influence could have contributed to a sense that he was beyond challenge.

“When you have a billion pounds in the bank, the achievements he has had and the power he has, you feel you’re unbreakable, you feel you’re unstoppable,” Neville added.

Despite his criticism, Neville does not believe the episode will define Ronaldo’s legacy permanently. He expects the attention to fade once the forward retires, allowing his achievements on the pitch to take centre stage again.

#2181. Is Mainoo-Tielemans the right midfield option for Man Utd?

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Manchester United drew 1-1 at home to Tottenham despite having an extra man advantage. The Reds saw Mbeumo put them in front, but Solanke scored a late leveller in injury time after Bentancur was shown a red card for headbutting Luke Shaw.

Manchester United’s worrisome season continues after accumulating just six points off the first six games, their first start in 40 years. But more than the results, what worries Manchester United is their midfield pairing.

United spent massively on their midfield rebuild and somehow can’t find the best combination in the midfield. Michael Carrick has gone with the Mainoo-Tielemans partnership, but they have failed to control the midfield. Individually, they both come with decent numbers, but collectively they fall short.

Mainoo and Tielemans struggled against Tottenham while closing the midfield and at times showed a lack of energy. On the day when nothing clicked for United, the Mainoo-Tielemans partnership particularly raised eyebrows.

Gary Neville has also raised serious concerns about Manchester United’s Mainoo-Tielemans partnership.

Neville was particularly unimpressed by the midfield balance, arguing that the problems went beyond one disappointing performance.

The former United captain stopped short of ruling out the combination altogether, but he suggested he has serious doubts about its suitability.

“Tielemans and Mainoo, I’m not calling time on it, that’s probably too drastic, but I’ve seen enough of it to think that’s not a midfield that’s dynamic enough to play at this football club in the Premier League.”

Former Spurs midfielder Redknapp felt United struggled to connect their midfield with the rest of the team during the opening stages.

He added: “I didn’t see enough connections in that midfield in the first hour. Baleba came on and did OK and showed a bit more.

“They look devoid of any confidence, any idea, any patterns of play, playing out from the back. Tottenham were much better than them in every single department.”

While Carlos Baleba was a particular highlight on the day, he showed great potential when brought on. He brought a new sense of energy in the midfield and sent a few balls over the top for a couple of quick counterattacks as well.

Redknapp also pointed to the physical demands of modern Premier League football, arguing that United cannot afford to be outworked by their opponents.

Tottenham ran a staggering 5 km more than Manchester United, something Redknapp felt was a pattern.

"Spurs ran 5km more than Man United… it's like that EVERY GAME!"Jamie Redknapp is less than impressed with the lack of ground covered this season by Manchester United 😬 pic.twitter.com/BxWkFuyGJ1

“There is a pattern that I don’t really like when I watch teams, and that’s when every time the stats don’t lie and the other team runs more than you.

“I feel in Premier League football right now there are a couple of non-negotiables, and one is you have to run more than the other team if you want to win or at least get close enough. Tonight Spurs ran 5km more than Manchester United.”

He believes Tottenham’s pressing exposed the lack of pace in United’s midfield, making it difficult for the hosts to move the ball safely through the centre.

“In terms of how they played today, I think they have an issue with Tielemans and Mainoo in midfield. I don’t see enough energy in there. Baleba will add a lot and will give them a tremendous amount of energy but right now, when things aren’t going well… and Tottenham knew it!

“Every time United played the ball out from the back they pounced. They pressed them because they knew United don’t have the pace and speed in midfield to get away.”

“In isolation, one of them is absolutely fine, the two of them together doesn’t work for me. They haven’t got the pace or the energy to get around the pitch for 90 minutes.”

#2180. Birmingham City told to consider 'no-brainer' transfer reunion with £15m man

Birmingham City have been told to consider a move for former St. Andrew's @ Knighthead Park favourite, Che Adams, ahead of the looming January transfer window.

Despite a lesser amount of activity in the summer market, Blues are firmly among the early-season candidates for promotion, with Chris Davies' tactical reshuffles in recent months certainly paying dividends.

As well as having a squad which has now adapted to the roughness of the Championship after coasting to the League One title two seasons ago, such changes have clearly got the best out of Davies' attackers.

August Priske has become one of the most feared strikers in the division based on his early-season form after signing in January, whilst Jay Stansfield looks far more assured in an attacking-midfield role at present.

There are question marks over what the future holds for Marvin Ducksch, though, after extended exit talk in the summer, whilst Luis Vazquez's start in B9 hasn't been overly convincing.

Adams, 30, is currently in his third season with Torino in Serie A, having left Southampton in 2024 following their return to the Premier League.

It has been seven years since the forward departed Birmingham for Saints in a £15m deal, though, but he hasn't lost sight of where the goal is, posting over 20 strikes for his current employers in just over 80 outings.

Given the experience the striker has got under his belt since leaving St. Andrew's, Football League World's Birmingham City fan pundit, Jason Moore, has urged the deal to be explored.

He told FLW: "I can probably sit and say, is he not going to play enough? Is he going to be happy with that?

"But, if we convinced him, and you could make the move happen, you'd be silly not to bring him in.

"He's a proven Premier League striker, granted he doesn't score tons. He's played in a very decent league in Italy. It'd be an absolute no-brainer to get Che Adams in," Moore stated.

"You need two decent players in every position and Jay is predominantly playing as a 10, so if you have Priske and someone like Che Adams as your strikers to get you up, it puts you in pretty good stead going forward, doesn't it?

Blues fans, should a reunion with Che Adams be prioritised in January? Comment below!

"I'd be 100% up for bringing Che Adams back and any Blues fan that wouldn't, I just think it would be silly. If you get to January, and you're still in the position of 'it looks like we might come up', every single team in the past few years have added that bit of power just to make sure they get up.

"Ipswich got Mehmeti in January just to make sure they went up. I think about when they brought Kieffer Moore in at the end of the season when they first went up, Benik Afobe at Wolves was another one. Clubs always do it, just add a little bit more firepower to get up," he explained.

"If we can go and get Che Adams in January to make sure we go up, I'm all for it."

The thought of bringing Adams back to the West Midlands is one which would certainly excite many Birmingham fans, especially after his recent sighting with former strike partner, Lukas Jutkiewicz.

However, speculation between the club and the 50-time Scotland international under Knighthead's ownership is nothing new at this point.

Indeed, it was claimed in January that Birmingham staff were not against the idea of a sentimental transfer, given the impact which Demarai Gray has had since returning to his home city on a free transfer.

Blues fans may be unaware that some high-profile names once associated with their club are still playing years later

As Moore mentions, sourcing a move for a forward who has scored 49 Championship goals in 156 appearances and won promotion would be a smart move for Blues if an agreement could be reached.

That said, Adams is featuring regularly for Torino this campaign, meaning a stumbling block may arise regardless of his feelings towards the Midlands club.

However, if a deal was reached, it could have some of their divisional rivals scratching their heads, as Wolves were linked with his services in the summer, and are currently lacking in attacking depth following an injury to Raul Jimenez two months ago.

#2179. One lesson Senne Lammens learned after Tottenham’s late equaliser

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Senne Lammens believes Manchester United must learn to finish games off after their late collapse against Tottenham Hotspur.

The result left the goalkeeper frustrated at Old Trafford despite leading the game and with a player more on the field.

Manchester United looked set for three points after Bryan Mbeumo put them ahead in the second half, but Dominic Solanke’s stoppage-time equaliser denied Michael Carrick’s side a much-needed victory.

Speaking to MUTV, Senne Lammens admitted the team had struggled early on but had done enough after the break to put themselves in a winning position.

“Football can be a bit cruel at times,” the Belgian goalkeeper said. “There are a lot of ups and downs, especially in a game like that.”

Senne Lammens felt United failed to take advantage of Tottenham’s red card, allowing the visitors too much space to find a late equaliser.

“We’ve won a lot of games against 10 men [in the past], but you also know there can be a tricky situation when you’re playing against 10 men, because they have nothing to lose and they know that.”

He added that United should have scored again to put the result beyond doubt or, at the very least, prevented Tottenham from creating further chances.

“I think that’s what we did wrong. We gave them a little bit too much breathing room to create those chances, and then one ball has to go right for them, one bounce has to go their way, and they can score like that.”

The goalkeeper also pointed to similarities with United’s earlier disappointment against Everton, where points slipped away late in the match.

“You feel like those games just slip away from you, and there are one or two situations where you don’t really have control,” he said.

“I think there’s a lot to learn from today. It wasn’t all negative, not at all, but especially the way it ended left a very bad feeling.”

“I think the easy answer is that we have to be able to kill the game faster and not put ourselves in that position to give them a chance.”

Despite the frustration, Lammens said there were positives to take from the performance. However, the late goal had left a bitter feeling, and United now need to turn those lessons into better results.

#2178. Manchester United defence: Why Carrick must trust Yoro and Heaven

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Manchester United endured another embarrassing draw against 10-man Tottenham after failing to register their second win despite bringing in a plethora of players.

Manchester United invested heavily last season in their attacking options, bringing in Bryan Mbeumo and Matheus Cunha.

This season, they overhauled their midfield. United spent massively on Carlos Baleba, Youri Tielemans and Andrey Santos. Despite spending massively, they have failed to produce the control they would have expected in the midfield. The lack of control was evident in the recent Tottenham game, where Mainoo-Tielemans partnership failed to control the game collectively.

The midfield question was covered in our recent report as well, highlighting a lack of control and carried comments from Gary Neville and Jamie Redknapp.

The bigger question is when United will strengthen their defence. As per our previous report as well, Manchester United are planning to overhaul their defence in the upcoming transfer windows.

United were heavily linked with several left backs but eventually failed to bring any one. The situation left Luke Shaw as the only senior option in the position, while Patrick Dorgu is used upfront by Michael Carrick.

Harry Amass is another option for Michael Carrick, but he is only here because of a failed transfer move. He has been rarely used at left back as Dalot and Mazraoui get the nod in the position ahead of him.

The situation at right-back isn’t all good either. Diogo Dalot is the first option there, but he rarely provides the assurance that puts Michael Carrick at ease. Noussair Mazraoui is the second choice at right-back, but his attacking numbers aren’t great either.

Harry Maguire and Lisandro Martinez form up United’s centre-back pairing while Leny Yoro and Ayden Heaven are used all over the defensive line in rotation when needed. Yoro was used at right-back against Tottenham while Heaven arrived as left-back towards the end of the game.

Manchester United’s season hasn’t been defensively strong or anywhere near being good. They have conceded goals in all Premier League games and only kept one clean sheet against Sabah in the Champions League.

Lisandro Martinez has come under scrutiny after errors on four occasions. Against Ipswich Town, Manchester United won 5-2, but his error resulted in Ipswich’s second goal.

His own goal against Fulham gave away two points at Craven Cottage in the 1-1 draw. Another error led to Solanke’s goal against Tottenham, when he took his eyes off the ball.

His injury concerns are another aspect, and maybe Michael Carrick needs to make a decision.

Manchester United’s success depended heavily on youth over the years. When pundits questioned them, Sir Alex Ferguson gave them the chance to prove them wrong. Alan Hansen made the famous quote “You can’t win anything with kids” on the BBC’s Match of the Day on August 19, 1995. United did exactly that, played youth and went on to win everything.

Michael Carrick needs to do exactly that. He needs to trust youth and allow them to make mistakes. Leny Yoro and Ayden Heaven arrived as massive potentials, but they haven’t been given the license to express themselves, without fearing for their places. Both the youngsters have rarely started together or in a stretch of games. It is maybe time for them to take their chances.

Michael Carrick needs to keep Harry Maguire as a senior experienced figure around them. There is no doubt over Martinez’s ability, but he is not the answer to United’s long-term problem. United need to blend youth with experience as their identity moving forward. Lisandro Martinez perhaps isn’t the answer to United’s problem.

A defensive revival expected in the upcoming transfer windows needs to happen now. It must happen with chances to the Youth. Michael Carrick needs to be brave enough and throw the youngsters in the mix. What it will bring is a long-term solution. They need to experience the errors and pressures that will shape them in football.

#2177. What Martin O'Neill told Kasper Hogh about Celtic goal drought after Besiktas

As German referee Sascha Stegemann sounded his full-time whistle, Martin O'Neill raised his right fist to the smoke-filled Glasgow sky.

Celtic's Europa League campaign was finally up and running courtesy of a frenetic 3-2 victory over Besiktas, the manager visibly relieved and triumphant in equal measure.

O'Neill usually heads straight up the tunnel after matches. Thursday evening was different. He instead made a beeline for his players before their customary walk round on the Celtic Park turf, embracing captain Callum McGregor and goalscorers Callum Osmand and Camilo Duran.

A turning point in Celtic's season? It's too early to say. But O'Neill knew for certain that failure to beat Besiktas would have rendered the remainder of Celtic's Europa League campaign meaningless. Now they have fresh hope and a platform for progress.

O'Neill insists they must build on their gutsy European victory.

"That is what we want to do, absolutely," O'Neill said. "From the European side, we have given ourselves a chance. We will have to go and win away from home and it will not be easy."

Thursday evening saw the manager make five changes to his lineup. Jordan Lotomba recorded his first start at right-back and Kasper Hogh, Sebastian Tounekti, and James Forrest made up a second string frontline.

From Forrest, O'Neill wanted experience and added defensive security. Hogh offered a focal point in attack and went off to a standing ovation in the 77th minute.

It marked his 15th Celtic appearance without a goal but the home crowd were appreciative of his work rate, as was O'Neill.

"I want centre-forwards to be able to hold it and that is what he did," O'Neill stated. "He is not Duran, he is not a dribbler. But if he can do this for us against strong opposition it will stand him in good stead.

"I think Thursday night will be good for him. I said to him that I’m not worried about whether he scores a goal.

"Of course you want centre-forwards to score and of course centre-forwards live on them. But I said to him that last night I wanted him to be strong and fight when the ball is played up to him.

"I thought in terms of holding the ball up and bringing people into the game that he did really well."

Hogh, signed from Bodo/Glimt for a club record transfer fee in excess of £11 million during the summer transfer window, had no opportunities against Besiktas.

The Dane registered just one touch in the opposition penalty area and didn't have a single shot on goal.

Hogh is being starved of the service upon which he thrived at Bodo.

To that concern, O'Neill responded: "If they are going man for man and we have gone really, really low in terms of going close to the goalkeeper then the next one in if you are dinking it in is where a centre-forward has to play.

"If it is a decent ball in then he has to hold it up because he should be first. If it is in the air and he has to fight for it then it is difficult. But if you dink it in for him then you can start from there."

"Centre-forwards need a goal," O'Neill continued. "But that performance there against European opposition will give him confidence. I don’t know what side we will play on Sunday. But this lad has scored goals in the Champions League remember.

"He will get a confidence boost from Thursday night in terms of doing that part of the game well. But it will be nice for him to score a goal."

Hogh needn't have to worry about goals if Duran maintains his apex form. The Colombian couldn't have been more influential after coming off the bench against Besiktas, netting a crucial brace to give Celtic all three points.

First, Duran latched on to Osmand's reverse pass, rounding opposition goalkeeper Alexander Nubel and slotting into an empty net. He then brilliantly controlled Kieran Tierney's wayward shot, this time beating Nubel from six yards out.

"At this minute, he is on fire," O'Neill said of Duran. "He can play wide right, he has done that. He has played wide left for his other club. But centre-forward is where he is dynamic."

When Duran plays through the middle, Hogh is often sacrificed. Conversely, Hogh starting usually sees Duran shifted to the right flank. For O'Neill, it's onerous finding the perfect balance.

"We made five changes on Thursday night because it was necessary," he explained. "The boys who came on were always going to come on after 60 minutes.

"You can’t just plan things like this. Somebody might pick up an injury. Things change during the course of the game. But the plan before it was for these lads to go on. We were always going to make the changes.

"It was necessary if we wanted a bit more gusto in the side and a bit more playing in behind. That gave the opportunity for Duran to weave a bit of magic."

Focus rightly lay on Celtic's attacking play after Thursday. Yet their defence was equally pivotal, limiting Besiktas to few opportunities of note.

Beyond a penalty and the resulting rebound, from which they scored, Vincenzo Italiano's side offered little in the way of forward penetration, struggling to break down Celtic's organised structure. It took a long-range effort from Orkun Kokcu to score their second of the evening.

"It was really pleasing and really important," O'Neill said of his team's defending. "We conceded some ground in the game which was to be expected, but I thought defensively we were really, really strong.

"When corners came in we dealt with them and we dealt with second balls as well after it was played out. The back four was terrific. Lotomba did well until he conceded the penalty."

Central defender Cameron Carter-Vickers had one of his better displays. The American made nine clearances and won all but one of his five duels. Accurate on the ball too, Carter-Vickers brought an air of stability to the backline.

He recovered ably from his performance against Falkirk last weekend, when his meek clearance led directly to Ethan Ross' opener. Carter-Vickers came off in the latter stages with a hamstring complaint, but O'Neill thinks he had a point to prove.

"It looked that way," said the Celtic boss. "I thought he was very strong. He was complaining about his hamstrings being very tight at half-time and I thought we would give him another 15 minutes.

"Of course, the game changed in that time and we were chasing the game from behind. But the lads who came on in the forward positions were always going to come on. James Forrest was never going to last the whole game.

"I thought he was outstanding, an absolute credit to the football club with what he did for us. Even defensively. Everybody worked so hard. It was really pleasing."

The key for Celtic and O'Neill is building on Thursday's thrilling victory. As the manager knows all too well, it'll count for nothing if Kilmarnock, St Mirren, and Torreense aren't beaten in the coming weeks.

#2176. He played Huddersfield Town & Luton - Then he was jailed over charity fraud

He made over 500 senior club appearances and played against both England and Argentina in the World Cup finals, but when Efe Sodje was found to have been siphoning off money from a charity in his name, none of that mattered in the slightest.

With his distinctive do-rag headband, Efe Sodje was a familiar face English football over the first decades of this century. Sodje had the sort of career that most EFL players could only dream of. Not only did he enjoy a twenty-year career which took in eleven clubs, but he was also recognised at international level.

Born in London to Nigerian parents, Sodje was picked for the Nigeria international team twelve times even though he never kicked a ball in the Premier League, and this included being selected for their national team squad for the 2002 World Cup finals, where he played against two former winners of the competition, both England and Nigeria.

Sodje brought his playing career to a close in 2015 at 33 years of age, but less than two years later he found himself in court on fraud charges and, after his conviction on those charges, ended up spending time at her majesty's pleasure.

Born in London in October 1972, Efe Sodje made his breakthrough in the senior game in the Football Conference (now the National League) in 1994. After three years at Broadhall Way, he left for newly-promoted Macclesfield Town in 1997, starting a spell in the EFL which would last for 16 years.

Sodje would make 303 appearances in the League over this time, during which he played for eight League clubs, including Luton Town, Huddersfield Town, Crewe Alexandra and Southend United. His longest spells came at Crewe, for whom he made 113 appearances, and Bury, for whom he made 212.

He won honours at six different clubs. In the non-league game, he was part of the Stevenage team which won the Football Conference title in 1996, but it was in the Football League that he picked up most of his silverware. He was promoted from the third tier with Southend United in 2006 - and was part of their team which knocked Manchester United out of the League Cup in November 2006 - and was promoted from the fourth tier no fewer than four times, with Macclesfield Town in 1998, Crewe Alexandra in 2003, Huddersfield Town in 2004 and Bury in 2011.

But he was perhaps best known for his surprise appearances for the Nigeria international team. While with Luton Town during the 1999-2000 season, he was called up to the national team squad for the 2000 African Cup of Nations, in which his country finished as runners-up to Cameroon, although he didn't play in the tournament.

But the bigger shock came two years later, when he wasn't just called up to the squad for the 2002 World Cup finals, but played against both Argentina and England, although his team was knocked out in the group stages of the competition. In total, Sodje - whose brother Sam also played international football for Nigeria - made 12 appearances for his country.

Sodje ended his playing career in mysterious circumstances, though. He played his last two seasons back in the National League with Barrow, and then back at Macclesfield. But when his departure from the game was announced in June 2016, by which time he was 42 years old, no reason was given for it.

Early in 2019, Sky Sports ran a story which may have explained Sodje's sudden departure from professional football. In September 2017, he - along with two of his brothers, Bright and Stephen - had been given jail sentences for their involvement in defrauding a charity which had been set up in their name.

It was reported that the three had siphoned off £63,000 which had been donated to the Sodje Sports Foundation, a charity ostensibly set up to provide aid for poor children in the Niger delta. Sky reported that, "The three brothers used their connections within professional football and business communities to secure support for their foundation and to give it an appearance of legitimacy."

Efe Sodje was imprisoned for 18 months, Bright Sodje for 21 months, and Stephen Sodje for two years and six months. As well as this, Sam Sodje - who himself made over 200 senior appearances for an array of clubs, including Brentford, Leeds United and Charlton Athletic - was acquitted, while Sam Sodje's brother Akpo fled the UK during the investigation, leading to the issuance of a European Arrest Warrant after he refused to return from Dubai over the investigations. He had previously been connected to a spot-fixing case while playing for Tranmere Rovers.

When handing down the sentences in January 2019, Judge Michael Topolski QC told the defendants: "You have brought shame upon yourselves and your family." Having been given the huge leg-up of the sort of sporting ability that many people would give anything for, it's impossible to avoid the conclusion that the judge was entirely right in his assessment of them.

#2175. Breaking: Man Utd begin formal progress for Michael Carrick replacement

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Manchester United are reportedly looking at options beyond Michael Carrick after the club believes he is not the right option for United.

Former Germany manager Julian Nagelsmann is among the names linked with a possible move to Old Trafford if United

Indykaila, a famous insider account on X, reported that Manchester United have started the process of finding their next head coach. Nagelsmann’s representatives are said to be willing to hear what the club has planned before deciding whether the project would suit the German manager.

United have not announced any change in their managerial plans, so the report remains unconfirmed. Carrick is still in charge, but his results at the start of the new season have given the club plenty to think about.

Michael Carrick did enough during his interim spell last season to earn the job permanently. After taking over following Ruben Amorim’s departure in January, he won 11 of his 16 matches and guided United to third place in the Premier League, securing Champions League qualification.

That run convinced the club to hand him a two-year contract in May. The new campaign, though, has been a disappointing story so far. United have picked up just six points from their first six league games, their worst start since 1985/86.

The 39-year-old’s representatives are reportedly open to hearing United’s plans, but there is no confirmed agreement or evidence that formal negotiations have taken place.

Carrick’s achievements last season bought him the permanent role, but the opening weeks of this campaign have left little room for further slip-ups.

#2174. Why James Morrison and West Brom fans are tipping 26-year-old star for a Premier League rise as impeccable form continues

West Brom sit 5th in the table after a 3-0 win over Millwall on Saturday made it six games without defeat. Isaac Price and Jayson Molumby made the headlines as they both scored one and set up another, but it was a fantastic team performance from WBA who strengthen their promotion credentials with every passing match.

Although not a regular on the scoresheet, one man never far away from praise is Ousmane Diakite. And, once again, the Malian midfielder was excellent on Saturday, playing the full 90 minutes in midfield as the Baggies stormed to victory.

Focusing on the win over Millwall, Diakite was key in his usual role of winning duels, guarding the backline and keeping the ball moving. Primarily operating on the left of the midfield two, the West Brom man completed 51 of his 58 passes, five of which were into the final third while one created a chance.

In duels is where the 26-year-old found most of his success though, as has been the case all season. Diakite won seven duels in total: six on the ground and one in the air. He also completed three tackles, made two interceptions and four recoveries (FotMob).

This latest effort contributed to his eye-catching totals across the Championship campaign to date. No central midfielder has won more than his 92 duels, and he ranks amongst the league’s best in his position for aerial duels won (28 – top 2%), clearances (44 – top 2%) and defensive contributions (98 – top 3%).

On the ball, WBA star Diakite has completed 595 passes (top 9%), made 86 line-breaking passes (top 23%), made 13 successful dribbles (top 8%) and won 23 fouls (top 4%). As far as his stats go, he has been one of the Championship’s most effective midfielders of the season so far in all key areas apart from chance creation and goalscoring with those responsibilities falling to Albion stars Price, Jimmy-Jay Morgan, Aune Heggebo and more.

After a challenging start in a West Brom shirt, Diakite proved his worth in last season’s relegation fight under James Morrison and deservedly earned a new deal. He has got better and better in the 2026/27 campaign, and is a crucial part of an Albion side that looks ready for a promotion challenge.

In the build up to the Millwall game, Baggies boss Morrison was asked whether Diakite was a Premier League player in the making. Speaking to reporter Steve Tudgay, he said: “Yes he is. With us!”

Similarly, former West Brom man Paul Robinson recently told the Express and Star: “He has Premier League attributes, when you look at him”.

Not only do Diakite’s stats back this up, but so do the rave reviews of WBA supporters. Over the past week or so, numerous fans have shared their belief in his potential on X:

Ousmane Diakite WILL be a premier league footballer. Hopefully with us but time will tell #wba

Diakite walks into a bottom half Prem team all day for me #wba

Diakite could get into the Man united midfield all day, his improvement since joining has been unreal to watch #wba

West Brom fans will be hoping Diakite’s form continues. If it does, he may well get his deserved step up to the top flight at The Hawthorns.

#2172. Rangers and Portsmouth swerved transfer bullet as Frank Lampard hit the buffers

Portsmouth head into the international break on an excellent run of form, as John Mousinho's side dare to dream of a place in the Championship eight come May.

Portsmouth, after a difficult start to the campaign where they won just two of their opening eight league games, have bounced back superbly, and instead of looking like relegation candidates, look like an outside shout for a play-off place.

Head coach Mousinho has done a superb job of taking Pompey from the depths of League One and cementing their status in the Championship, as now, the 40-year-old is aiming to take them a level higher.

Recruitment, on the whole, has been exceptional at Fratton Park over recent years despite a lack of funds, as Portsmouth may well have dodged an expensive bullet courtesy of their lack of financial muscle.

Under the ownership of Eisner's, Portsmouth have never spent beyond their means; instead, they have recruited smartly by uncovering undervalued talent across the globe.

Nicolas Schmid joined from Blau Weiss Linz in Austria, Adrian Segecic arrived from Sydney FC in Australia, Rocco Shein from Fredrikstad in Norway, and Marko Milovanovic headed to Fratton Park from Spanish outfit Almeria.

Whilst this summer has seen Pompey splash the cash a bit more than what they have been used to, just six months ago was not a similar story, having lost out on the capture of Surinamese winger Jahnoah Markelo from Swiss outfit FC Zurich to Coventry City.

Mousinho revealed he was interested in signing the winger during the January window in April 2026, but cited his reasoning for not completing the deal as the hefty fee, as Markelo headed to then-division rivals Coventry for around £4.3 million instead.

Football League World has asked AI to rank the top 25 most demanding fanbases in British football right now

Following his move to the CBS Arena, Markelo found himself competing for minutes alongside the likes of Romain Esse, Min-hyeok Yang, Tatsuhiro Sakamoto, and Ephron Mason-Clark, with Brandon Thomas-Asante also offering an option out wide.

Markelo came through the ranks of FC Twente before spending time with Go Ahead Eagles, NK Kustosija Zagreb, NK Celje, and FC Zurich before his move to Coventry, but what looked to be an exciting signing at the time quickly proved to be a confusing one.

The 23-year-old was only included in eight of 17 Championship matchday squads he was available for, playing just a total of 44 minutes across five substitute appearances for the Sky Blues, who would eventually earn promotion to the Premier League via the title, to little impact from Markelo.

Following Coventry's promotion, it was already apparent that Markelo did not have a big role to play under Lampard, and heading up to a division likely meant even fewer opportunities would be afforded to him, and so that proved to be.

Just six months after signing for Coventry for around £4.3 million, a permanent exit was on the cards for the winger, as Scottish giants Rangers reportedly opened talks with the Sky Blues over a deal for Markelo.

However, the Surinamese instead opted to head to the Middle East with United Arab Emirates-based outfit Shabab Al-Ahli, where he has further failed to set the world alight.

Markelo has made just eight appearances for the club to date across all competitions, failing to register a single goal contribution, as right now, it looks like Portsmouth and Rangers have dodged a bullet with this deal.

Do you think Portsmouth and Rangers have dodged a bullet by not signing Markelo, or would things have gone differently had he headed to either of those instead? Share your thoughts below!

With Markelo's lack of impact at either Coventry or Shabab Al-Ahli in the past 11 months or so, it's looking increasingly likely that both Portsmouth and Rangers have gotten away with one here.

Had Pompey had the funds to rival the Sky Blues for Markelo's signature back in January 2026, he may well have headed to Fratton Park instead, though whether he would have had better fortunes under Mousinho remains to be seen.

As it stands, the Surinamese has failed to justify his £4.3 million transfer fee, as Portsmouth and Rangers must both be relieved.

#2171. Why must Manchester United move beyond the Glazers?

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

The Glazers took control of Manchester United in 2005. That takeover has since caused so many disruptions and protests by the fans over massive debts and insufficient backing of the team in the transfer market, despite successful periods in the Premier League and Europe.

Now Manchester United need to think about who should control the club in the future. Laurie Whitwell has now shared his views on why Manchester United need an ownership change. He simply wants the Glazers gone from Old Trafford. However, he also recognizes that replacing the current ownership doesn’t automatically mean things will improve for United.

The journalist believes the change in ownership has had a lasting effect on United, with the club still feeling its consequences today. A fresh start might be welcome, but the identity of the next owners would matter just as much as the departure of the existing ones.

“Personally, I would like to see the Glazers gone from Manchester United,” Laurie Whitwell said on The Rest is Football podcast with Gary Lineker.

“That change, you know, just really affected and still impacts United now.”

The desire for different ownership is one thing, but finding people capable of taking the club forward is another.

Whitwell admitted there is no perfect answer when it comes to who should take charge.

That leaves United facing a difficult choice. A sale could offer the chance to change how the club is run, but simply bringing in a new owner would not guarantee better decisions or a brighter future.

Whitwell also raised the idea of United supporters having a greater say in the club’s ownership and decisions, much like the Barcelona model where fans own the club itself. However, he highlighted that the Barcelona model isn’t ideal either and has its own shortcomings.

Changing the names at the top would only be one part of the change. The club would also need an ownership structure capable of making sound decisions and providing a direction.

The issue of ownership goes beyond whether the Glazers should stay or leave. Manchester United needs to find the right people and the right model to secure the club’s future. Getting that decision wrong could leave them facing the same circus the fans are enduring under the Glazers.

#2170. Fabrizio Romano shares latest on Martinez contract after explosive rant

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

The Argentinian defender has also apologized to the United higher ups as well over his comments, as shared by Fabrizio Romano.

Lisandro Martinez has held discussions with Jason Wilcox and Michael Carrick after speaking out following United’s 2-0 defeat to Atletico Madrid.

Martinez had questioned whether Carrick was backed enough in the summer and suggested the club needed changes.

Fabrizio Romano has now confirmed that United consider the matter resolved for now and expect Martinez to remain available for selection.

Speaking about the situation on his YouTube channel, as quoted by Football 365, Romano explained why Martinez’s comments caused concern inside the club.

“He spoke very strongly after the game against Atletico Madrid, which Man Utd lost. It was a very negative moment for the Red Devils. We know that Licha was very strong in his comments. He said that Michael Carrick was not backed enough during the summer transfer window.”

Romano added that Martinez had spoken directly to those at the club after the incident.

“Licha apologised to Man Utd as a club. He spoke to the directors, he spoke to Jason Wilcox, and he spoke to Michael Carrick, who was also involved in this conversation.”

“So, the underlying message remains the same. Lisandro is apologising for the way he expressed himself after a game during a difficult moment for Man Utd, but there was a discussion about the substance of what he said.”

Romano also revealed that United had imposed a financial penalty on the centre-back. However, the punishment does not appear to involve removing him from the squad.

“According to the information available tonight, Lisandro Martinez has been fined.”

He added that Martinez is expected to return to the squad as normal, with the fine considered sufficient punishment for the incident.

“The internal discussion also helped to resolve all the issues. For Man Utd, the case is closed.”

That should bring some immediate clarity to the situation, although questions remain about how the episode could affect Martinez’s longer-term position at Old Trafford.

Romano also pointed towards Martinez’s contract, which expires next summer, with United holding an option to extend it.

“So, let’s see what is going to happen there. For sure, this is the kind of situation that will have an impact on the situation surrounding Lisandro, but we have to see what happens next in terms of future transfers and everything else.”

#2166. Leeds vs Man Utd preview: Team news, form, injuries and where to watch

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Leeds United welcome Manchester United to Elland Road on Sunday, with both sides looking to put a difficult run of results behind them and get their Premier League season on track.

Leeds United suffered a 2-1 defeat away to Arsenal last weekend despite James Justin scoring to put them in front in the 55th minute.

Manchester United also dropped points in the Premier League, drawing 1-1 with Tottenham, before losing 2-0 to Atletico Madrid in the Champions League away from home.

Michael Carrick’s side have struggled to find consistency in recent weeks and have come under immense pressure to get in results. The Whites meanwhile will hope that home advantage can make the difference when United face their bitter rivals on Elland Road.

Leeds have recorded one win, two draws and two defeats in their last five matches in all competitions. Their best result came against Newcastle, where they won, but a 6-3 defeat to Chelsea in the Carabao Cup and the loss to Arsenal have left them searching for a response.

Manchester United have also won just once in their last five matches. Their 4-0 Champions League victory over Sabah FK was the only comfortable win, but defeats to Manchester City and Atletico Madrid, alongside draws against Fulham and Tottenham, have raised questions about their form and Michael Carrick.

Leeds are waiting on Dan James, who returned from international duty with a back problem and missed the Arsenal game. Joe Rodon remains sidelined with a hamstring injury and is not expected back in full training until next month. Harry Wilson could return after being an unused substitute last weekend. Mateo Joseph is also among Leeds’ absentees.

Manchester United have their own concerns. Luke Shaw and Kobbie Mainoo are doubts after picking up issues against Tottenham and missing Tuesday’s trip to Madrid. There is better news regarding Matthijs de Ligt, who could return to contention. Leny Yoro is also available for selection despite his Champions League suspension.

#2165. Barnsley questioned for ‘really odd’ ticket decision v Sheffield Wednesday

This article is part of Football League World's 'Terrace Talk' series, which provides personal opinions from our FLW Fan Pundits regarding the latest breaking news, teams, players, managers, potential signings and more…

By the time Barnsley host Sheffield Wednesday on the 20th November, the Tykes will be hopeful that they enter the Yorkshire derby with some added confidence.

The Tykes have had a torrid start to the season under Daniel Stendel, sitting joint-bottom of the third tier standings, taking just nine points from their opening 12 league games.

Bromley are the only side below Barnsley by having a 10-worst goal difference, so results need to pick up quickly if they are to avoid the dreaded League Two trapdoor.

The Reds ended a six-game straight losing run by drawing 1-1 with promotion contenders Oxford United last weekend, and before their league clash against the Owls, host Aldershot Town in the first round of the FA Cup, while tthe following midweek sees them return to Oakwell to face Oldham Athletic in the EFL Trophy.

In an ideal scenario, Barnsley will be hoping to earn back-to-back victories, which would then stand them in great stead for what was expected to be a raucous Oakwell against Wednesday.

However, there are fears now that Oakwell may not be as full and lively due to the club's ticket prices.

Barnsley v Sheffield Wednesday is the Friday night football on the 20th November, with Oakwell hosting a Yorkshire derby.

But there’s been criticism aimed at Barnsley for their ticket pricing for the fixture.

It was announced in the last few days that the Tykes are charging £35 for a standard adult ticket against the Owls, with some viewing that as extortionate, given the game is being broadcast live on Sky Sports.

Barnsley's ticket prices for the visit of Sheffield Wednesday have NOT gone down well...

Along with the adult ticket pricing, seniors (65+) are charged at £25, young adults (19-21) are also set at £25, while under-19s are £16 and under-14s are set at £10.

With this in mind, FLW’s Barnsley fan pundit has given his thoughts over the decision to charge adults £35 a ticket.

Speaking to Football League World, Barnsley fan pundit Andy Symcox has questioned the Tykes about their decision to charge adults £35 a ticket for their meeting with Sheffield Wednesday, believing the pricing is ‘really odd’ and will keep some fans away from Oakwell for such an important clash.

Andy told FLW: “For me, the decision to charge £35 for a matchday ticket at Barnsley versus Sheffield Wednesday, on the surface, seems really odd.

“The game itself is on Sky, and thus, how many fans will decide not to go to Oakwell to watch it on their TV’s instead, quoting the excessive price.

Barnsley fans, do you agree with our fan pundit's thoughts? What do you think of the adult ticket price against Sheffield Wednesday? Comment below!

“However, I wonder whether or not this is part of the reciprocal arrangement, as Sheffield Wednesday have notoriously charged very high prices in the past for games at Hillsborough, so much so that I personally refuse to go there due to the very high cost of tickets.

“On balance, however, given that it is on Sky, I find it a poor decision.”

A massive problem with football throughout the country is pricing, with tickets getting more and more unaffordable as the cost of living gets tougher and tougher.

And Barnsley are offering little help to their supporters through their latest price sting, with some supporters simply unable to pay such prices for a League One standard football match.

Five players, five careers. Name them from the clubs they played for.

Each day there are five current or former EFL players to identify. For each one you’ll see their senior clubs in the order they played for them. Loan spells are left out.

You get four guesses per player. Every wrong guess unlocks a clue: first their position, then their nationality, then their initials.

Get it first time for 4 points, then 3, 2 and 1. The best possible score is 20. A new set of players arrives at midnight.

In what should have been a fiery cauldron of noise given it’s a local derby, Barnsley will see fewer supporters at Oakwell on that Friday evening, with some electing to watch it on TV given it’s being broadcast by Sky Sports.

Because of this, Barnsley are driving their supporters away from a game that has the potential to turn their season right around, and they will only have themselves to blame come that night if Oakwell has a soulless atmosphere and contributes towards yet another negative performance and result.

In what is such a tough situation for the club, they need every single one of their supporters to be right behind the team and creating an atmosphere that makes Oakwell a fortress again, but the club are currently killing any chance of that by charging supporters far too much money.

It reinforces the sad state that football is in at the minute, with clubs failing in their duties to make football an affordable escape.

#2162. He played for West Brom & Bolton Wanderers - Then he was jailed over match-fixing plot

For 16 years, Delroy Facey was a familiar face in the EFL, but shortly after his playing career came to an end, shocking revelations were made about him which ended up with the former player in prison.

Over the course of a career that lasted for almost two decades, Delroy Facey made over 450 appearances in the senior game and was largely considered a stalwart player of the EFL's three divisions. He also represented Grenada twelve times at an international level, and even made ten Premier League appearances for Bolton Wanderers.

But shortly after his career ended, it became clear that he had been involved in a match-fixing scandal which tarnished his entire legacy as a player, and which resulted in him being sent to prison for two and a half years, along with a number of others in a scandal which had involved the National Crime Agency, the Gambling Commission and the Football Association, following two separate newspaper investigations.

Born in Huddersfield in 1980, Delroy Facey joined his hometown team as an apprentice at 16-years-old and signed his first senior contract with the club the following year. By the 1998-99 season, he was breaking into their first team.

His playing career became somewhat nomadic. In total, he played for ten different clubs and spent time on loan at three others, meaning that, although he ended up making 451 senior appearances in the EFL and Premier League - where he played ten times for Bolton Wanderers between 2002 and 2004 - between 1997 and 2012, the 85 times he played for Huddersfield was the most that he played for any one club.

The only silverware of his career came at the end of the 2009-10 season. He'd joined Notts County in 2008 and became something of a fan favourite, and in 2010 he was part of their team which won the League Two title, although he was released by the club at the end of that season.

He also found a route international game as a result of his success at club level. He qualified to play for Grenada, Trinidad & Tobago and Jamaica through his parents and was called up to the Grenada squad in June 2009. Between then and 2011, he made twelve appearances for the Grenada national team.

Football League World looks at ten former EFL players to have served time behind bars...

The scandal broke in December 2013. Separate investigations carried out by The Sunday Times and The Sun on Sunday had revealed a spot-fixing scandal within the EFL, leading to the arrest of six people, including Facey. Spot-fixing - the practice of gambling on any aspect of a match apart from the final result - had become a signficant issue in line with the growth of online gambling, which allowed a wider range of options for betting than had ever been seen before.

It was reported that Sam Sodje, a former player for Reading and Portsmouth, claimed that he could arrange for footballers in the Football League Championship to get themselves booked in exchange for cash payments. It was also reported that Sodje "was filmed describing how he had twice punched an opponent, Oldham Athletic's Jose Baxter, while playing for Portsmouth in a League One match last February as he sought to receive a red card in exchange for £70,000."

Facey was charged with conspiracy to bribe alongside Moses Swaibu, a non-league player who'd played for a number of clubs including Lincoln City and Bromley. The case went to trial in the middle of April 2015. The court heard that Facey had told a footballer at Hyde FC that he could make some "easy money" by fixing the result of a match, and had also told a contact that some Football Conference - now the National League - teams would "do" a game in return for payment.

And on the 29th April 2015, the verdict came in. Delroy Facey was convicted of conspiracy to bribe and sentenced to two and a half years in prison. The BBC reported at the time that, after the sentencing, "Andy Young, from the CPS, said Facey had acted as the "middleman", sourcing players who would be willing to fix matches." Swaibu was also convicted and sentenced to 16 months in prison.

The temptation to lower division and non-league footballers is clear. The very nature of spot-fixing can make it difficult to prove, and the very existence of it is a direct result of the proliferation of online gambling in the first decade of the 21st century. The FA tightened their rules regarding players and gambling in August 2014, but for Delroy Facey the damage had been done. His reputation was in tatters, with the memories of him as a player fundamentally tarnished by the knowledge that he'd been involved in such disgraceful behaviour at the time.

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Manchester United travel to Italy on Wednesday, looking for their first win since September 10, when they face Como in the Champions League.

Michael Carrick’s side have struggled to build any momentum this season. Sunday’s 1-1 draw against Leeds extended their winless run to six matches in all competitions, leaving United with just seven points from their opening seven Premier League games.

The pressure on Carrick continues to grow. A win in Europe would offer some relief, but Como will not make life easy for Manchester United.

Manchester United began their Champions League campaign with a convincing 4-0 win over Sabah FK but lost 2-0 to Atletico Madrid in their next match. They have since drawn with Leeds, having also failed to beat Fulham and Tottenham in the Premier League.

#2158. Spurs included one Ash Phillips clause that Middlesbrough won’t stop thinking about

Middlesbrough are looking to end a 10-year exile from the Premier League at the end of this season, and are certainly well-placed to do so based on their early-campaign form.

Kim Hellberg was appointed at the Riverside Stadium 12 months ago, but has completely transformed the outlook of the North East side.

The Swede is now one of the most highly-rated head coaches outside the Premier League, whilst many of his squad on Teesside are in similar boats due to their current ability and exceptionally high potential.

Having fallen short in last season's automatic promotion race before losing May's play-off final to Hull City in crushing circumstances, Hellberg was significantly backed by Steve Gibson in the summer to make the desired changes in order to ensure such feats were a one-off.

As many as 12 new signings were recruited by Boro throughout the summer window, with depth and quality being added in equal measure right throughout their squad.

However, three of said dealings were particularly eye-catching in the form of Radek Vitek, Ash Phillips and Will Lankshear, with the latter duo both arriving in big-money moves from Tottenham Hotspur.

Spurs sold Will Lankshear to Boro in the summer, but, they have clauses written into the contract to protect their position in the future.

Phillips is no stranger to the Championship despite being just 21 years of age, having risen through the academy ranks at Blackburn Rovers before featuring 14 times for the Ewood Park outfit.

Spurs then activated a £2m release clause three summers ago, but the centre-back was swiftly loaned to Plymouth Argyle for the second period of 2023/24, with the Devon side narrowly avoiding relegation to League One.

Phillips then spent the past two seasons on loan at Stoke City, racking up 83 appearances in North Staffordshire, with his form and situation in North London subsequently attracting Middlesbrough's attention, whilst the Potters also sought a permanent move.

However, after a long-standing summer saga, during which talks were 'advanced' before hitting the buffers, the Salford native penned a five-year contract at the Riverside Stadium on August 15th.

Despite being so highly-rated, Phillips never made a senior appearance for the North London club, albeit the managerial shake-up which followed his arrival is likely to have contributed to such factors.

Roberto De Zerbi opted to cash in on the England youth international, who would otherwise be competing against the likes of Jan Paul van Hecke, Marcos Senesi and Micky van de Ven for a starting berth.

That said, Middlesbrough will certainly be wary of the fact Spurs still stand a chance of making their own significant funds if Phillips reaches his expected potential.

The aforementioned £7m initial deal is followed up by £13m in add-ons, meaning Spurs could make as much as £20m regardless, whilst also inserting a 20 percent sell-on clause within the agreement.

Spurs also have matching rights inserted into the deal, allowing them the potential to reunite with Phillips, irrespective of the fee another club puts on Middlesbrough's desk.

It remains to be seen what the future holds for Phillips, who has already become a mainstay in Hellberg's new-look defence as Middlesbrough set their sights on promotion.

The defender, no doubt, will eventually hope to make his mark in the Premier League too, and the Boro bosses' recent verdict only backs up why Spurs looked to protect themselves during negotiations.

"The thing for him is, and that's why I think he's here, because the way we play challenges him to do the things he needs to do to become a Premier League centre-back.

"I don’t think he is at the moment, but I think in some aspects of the game, he's up with the top, top, top level," he added.

#2156. Project 2035 sounds good but Scottish women’s football needs more than bold promises

A hefty dose of cynicism is always healthy whenever a politician turns up to kick a ball about with a group of kids for the cameras.

John Swinney got to work on his keepie-uppie skills this week at Easter Road as Project 2035 was rolled out in an initiative between the Scottish government and the SFA, among others, with the long-term ambitions of the project comfortably ticking the “ambitious in outlook” box.

It would be hard to find fault with the bold objectives that have been set for the next decade.

There were the immediate headline takeaways; doubling the number of registered female players in the country and quadrupling attendances at matches jumped out. Key, too, is regular qualification for major tournaments – and making an impact when qualification has been secured.

In terms of a manifesto that is exciting and visionary, there was much to be pleased about. But, as ever, the proof will be in the pudding. And when it comes to past behaviours predicting future outcomes, it would have to be said Scotland have excelled at taskforces and not quite so much at outcomes.

The initial outlay from the government is £250,000 for this year. That will cover the first step of their contribution but across the remaining terms until 2035 – by which time Scotland will hope to be co-hosts at the Women’s World Cup – the reality is that if those ambitions are to come to fruition, then pockets will need to be pretty deep.

It will take a lot of work and a lot of joined-up thinking across the board but the key to it all, as ever, is money.

The reality just now is this is a Scotland national team who have not qualified for a major tournament since 2019. There has been a regression in the SWPL both in terms of attendances, media exposure, quality and standard of players.

The league itself is split in terms of those who can afford to offer full-time professional contracts and those who can pay a tenner a week for expenses. A full-time professional SWPL may well be the first major step towards any quest to take the country to a level where it is fulfilling the objectives that were set out this week.

As it stands, that possibility would seem to be light years away.

It is not all doom and gloom. Scotland coach Melissa Andreatta has injected more than just enthusiasm and positivity into the national set-up. Initial impressions of her impact are positive, as witnessed by an unbeaten Nations League campaign and a return to League A.

Brazil next summer is the big one. Getting past Sweden in a two-legged World Cup play-off final next month would be the biggest result in Scotland’s history. It is fair to say it is a monumental challenge that lies ahead.

Caroline Weir has been shortlisted in successive years for the Ballon d’Or while Erin Cuthbert captains Chelsea in the WSL. Kim Little’s time with the national team has long since passed but that trio provide compelling evidence that Scotland have been able to produce world-class players.

But as the game has developed at a rate of knots elsewhere, it has stagnated in Scotland.

Lessons have to be learned from 2019, from the way that 18,555 fans turned up to wave Scotland off to France for their first World Cup to a collapse in interest and exposure within months. To understand how to get it right, there needs to be an understanding of where it went wrong.

The approach from government bodies to work in tandem with the SFA is sensible. The benefits of women retained in sport, not even at elite level but simply a recreational level, have a huge societal knock-on effect.

But it will need more than platitudes in front of cameras. The real work will be done in the nitty-gritty, day-to-day work. And elevating Scotland to the levels spoken about this week ultimately requires significant investment.

Megan Rapinoe has always been Marmite-like; observers either love her frank outspokenness or find their hackles rising on the back of it.

There are some arguments to suggest a woman with strong opinions she isn’t afraid to share will always invite a backlash. In any case, Rapinoe, so central for so long to such a successful USWNT as she claimed two World Cups and an Olympic goal medal, has been consistent that she sees no issue with trans athletes, a conversation that never seems to be too far from the surface in women’s sport.

Rapinoe’s argument centred on the idea there is no fairness in sport, citing the natural advantages of 7-foot-4-inch basketball player Victor Wembanyama as her example.

The problem with that comparison, however, is it fundamentally misses the point that biological males have a distinct advantage when it comes to competing.

Kelly Clark found herself out of Celtic’s starting line-up across recent weeks. The first Celtic women’s captain to lead the club to a title and the first to take them

into the Champions League, one suspects that finding herself on the bench would have been a sobering experience, and novel given the fact

she has been a mainstay of this Celtic team.

Drafted back into the starting XI, she produced a big goal for Grant Scott’s side on Wednesday night as she gave the Parkhead side a win over Hibs at Meadowbank.

Plenty of life in her yet, it would seem.
