#2127. ‘It affected my family’ – VAR official reveals full impact of huge decision which cost Liverpool

The official who was on VAR duty for the wrongly disallowed Luis Diaz goal in Liverpool’s 2-1 defeat to Tottenham in 2023 has spoken about the impact that the controversy had on his family.

The Colombian thought he had put the Reds 1-0 up in the 35th minute of the game in north London, but he was incorrectly flagged for offside, and a subsequent review from Stockley Park saw that erroneous decision upheld.

When the audio from the VAR conversation was publicly released a few days later, it was revealed that Darren England – the official on duty – had given the message of ‘check complete’ to the on-field referee in the mistaken belief that the goal was allowed to stand.

The 40-year-old has given an interview to BBC Sport in which he reflected on the regret from that hugely controversial decision, and how the fallout seeped into his family life.

England recalled: “Once I realised I’d made the error, it’s a really difficult place to try to forget that and move on. I can’t just physically stand up and walk out and leave it alone. A lot of resilience was needed – a lot of experience as well.”

He added: “The aftermath obviously was really difficult to deal with. It was challenging because it’s the goldfish bowl of the Premier League, but when mistakes happen, then you’re in the public eye and criticism’s inevitably going to come your way.

“That was difficult because it affected my family as well. We had people outside my girls’ school, journalists and things like that. That then affects you not as a referee, but as a human being.”

Want more Empire of the Kop coverage? Add us as a preferred source on Google to your favourites list for news you can trust

The catalogue of errors which led to Diaz’s legitimate goal being disallowed was farcical, with all of the officials on duty collectively culpable for the shambolic process which unfolded.

England did acknowledge in the VAR audio that he thought the on-field decision was onside and immediately held his hands up to the error, but that came as no comfort to anyone associated with Liverpool after such a costly and preventable outcome.

🚨 OFFICIAL: PGMOL have released VAR audio from Spurs v Liverpool 🔊 pic.twitter.com/zNLDyHkBbe

— Football on TNT Sports (@footballontnt) October 3, 2023

Criticism of the officials botching the process was inevitable and justified, but obviously the media intrusion into the referee’s family life crossed an unacceptable line, and we can empathise with how difficult the fallout must’ve been for the now 40-year-old.

Unfortunately, more than three years on from that chaotic blunder, refereeing standards in the Premier League haven’t improved to any great extent, as we saw at the weekend with the inexplicable decision not to award a penalty for a clear pull by Jurrien Timber on Alexander Isak’s shirt.

Pro Ref chief executive Howard Webb needs to do much more to ensure that a satisfactory standard of officiating is reached, but no person deserves to have an error in the course of the work turn into an unwarranted invasion on their family life.

If fewer errors made, then these intrusions would not happen. Ok, some marginal decisions will always be contested but these are usually forgotten rapidly as there are so many of them that attention moves to the next one, but there are far too many blatant errors. Why does VAR work in rugby without major controversy and yet fails miserably in football?

#2126. Chelsea sweat on key stars as Bournemouth test looms

Lucie Turner writes about Arsenal for Read Arsenal and Newcastle United for Read Newcastle. A long-time Arsenal supporter, she also follows rugby, snooker, darts and Formula 1. Her work covers match previews and reports, breaking news, player profiles and opinion pieces.

Chelsea return to Premier League action this weekend with a growing treatment table and a lot of questions for Xabi Alonso to answer before Saturday’s visit of Bournemouth.

The main concern revolves around Joao Pedro. Chelsea’s current player of the season rescued a point against newly promoted Hull, but he suffered a knock and sat out the damaging 3-0 defeat at Brentford. The problem also prompted his removal from Brazil’s squad shortly after he had returned to it.

It is understood that he is undergoing specialist knee treatment in an attempt to expedite his recovery, although reports in Brazil suggest that he could be out for up to four weeks. No date has yet been set, but a return later in October looks most likely.

There is more good news elsewhere. Cole Palmer was among the players called up to the latest England squad, which did not go down too well with Thomas Tuchel.

The midfielder has been recovering from a persistent groin injury and has been working with a rehab coach. He is likely to be fit this weekend.

Moises Caicedo has also struggled with an injury this season, playing just 14 minutes across the campaign. He’s spent the majority of time on the bench recovering from a bad calf injury, which ruled him out for most of the season.

He made his only appearance as a substitute against his former club Brighton, but the game was red-flagged for Ecuador’s friendlies, so he did not play.

Alonso said before the Brentford match that the player would be ready to return. It seems to be the case, unless something went wrong.

Captain Reece James missed the Brentford game with yet another hamstring problem. Alonso said he only just missed out, so a return seems likely.

There may also be debuts. Marco Palestra, the £27m signing from Atalanta, has been sidelined since a training injury just before the opening match against Fulham. He is back in full training and was expected to feature in a behind-closed-doors friendly with Portsmouth. Chelsea lost 2-0. Emmanuel Emegha, injured after scoring against Bromley in July, is also pushing for involvement.

Less clear is the position of Jamie Gittens. He limped off with an ankle injury playing for England Under-21s against Kazakhstan and was seen on crutches. Mike Penders is fine after a quad scare on Belgium duty. Returning to the bench against Turkey.

Chelsea badly need a lift. Defeat at Brentford before the break left Alonso’s side looking shaky, and a squad stretched by injuries has not helped.

Saturday brings a chance to steady things. If Palmer, James and Caicedo are available, with Palestra and Emegha potentially adding fresh options, the picture will look much brighter at Stamford Bridge. Joao Pedro’s absence will still sting. However, a strong response against Bournemouth would go a long way towards easing the pressure and restoring belief.

#2125. Arsenal injury news: Eberechi Eze concern emerges as Bukayo Saka awaits scan

Lucie Turner writes about Arsenal for Read Arsenal and Newcastle United for Read Newcastle. A long-time Arsenal supporter, she also follows rugby, snooker, darts and Formula 1. Her work covers match previews and reports, breaking news, player profiles and opinion pieces.

Arsenal came out on top in their match against Liverpool. Mikel Arteta admitted they have a new injury concern to deal with after Eberechi Eze was hurt. Bukayo Saka is also still out for the Gunners as they face two more games before the break.

The 1-0 victory over Liverpool at Anfield means they are three points clear at the top of the Premier League. Arteta will need to be careful with his squad ahead of their Champions League clash against Slavia Prague on Wednesday and home game against Hull City.

Eze was a surprise omission from the starting line-up, despite featuring well in recent games.

Arteta later revealed that the England international was suffering from a problem going into the game. He also praised the club’s medical and performance team for getting him fit enough to be included in the travelling party.

There is some light at the end of the tunnel, with Eze being named on the bench. There is uncertainty over whether he will be available for the trip to Prague.

Arsenal will look to train him to see if he can play against Slavia. However, Arteta’s comments suggest that the issue is significant enough that he couldn’t start against Anfield despite his recent form.

There is positive news on Declan Rice, Noni Madueke and Riccardo Calafiori. All three were able to overcome their fitness concerns to start the game against Liverpool and play the full 90 minutes.

The availability of these players provides Arteta with more options as he heads into a busy week. However, he will be keen to avoid any unnecessary risk to those that have only just returned to full fitness.

Saka is still the biggest injury concern for Arsenal as he has failed to play for the last three games after suffering a quad injury in the clash against Bayern Munich.

He was forced off after just 10 minutes of the Champions League draw. He will not be available for the fixtures against Slavia Prague and Hull.

There were concerns that Saka may miss up to eight weeks of action. Arteta hasn’t confirmed when he will be back on the pitch.

The Arsenal manager explained earlier that there was a need to scan Saka again to see how the fluid around the injury was behaving. The medical staff will also want to know if there has been any damage to the tendon.

This will give clearer information on when he will be able to return to play. Until then Arsenal will have to plan without one of their most important attacking players.

Another player looking to make his way back to fitness is William Saliba. The defender has not played this season after aggravating a back issue during his time playing for France at the World Cup.

However, he has avoided surgery and has been back in individual outdoor training at the club’s training ground recently. Arteta says there are positive signs that his recovery is going well.

The club plan for an MRI scan to see how he is recovering. They are hoping to get him back in December, but it has not been confirmed by Arsenal.

It would give Arteta a huge boost to his defensive options. For now he will have to keep monitoring his recovery.

They play Slavia Prague on Wednesday and then play Hull at home on Saturday. This gives them two more chances to build up their position ahead of the international break; however, Arteta will have to consider the latest medical reports.

#2124. Celtic hero Ramon Vega on how Scotland can help him save football from Infantino

For the vast majority of people in Scotland, the name of Ramon Vega will chiefly evoke memories of his short but successful spell at the heart of a treble-winning Celtic defence in the early 2000s. But might they soon know him as the saviour of FIFA, and even – as is his aim – the saviour of football’s soul?

The man himself would like to think so. For over a decade, he has been painstakingly preparing and positioning himself for a run at the FIFA presidency, and now, with trust in the organisation arguably at an all-time low under the leadership of Gianni Infantino, he believes that his time has come.

If faith is to be restored in football as a sport - never mind FIFA as a governing body - after Infantino presided over the highest World Cup ticket prices in history and then attempted to sell a 20 per cent stake in a new commercial entity controlling the rights to FIFA tournaments, including the World Cup, to private investors without consulting the FIFA Council, he is firmly of the mind that Infantino has to resign.

As it stands, Infantino intends to seek another term as FIFA president in March next year, a prospect that is, for Vega, unthinkable.

“Speaking frankly, the trust is completely gone,” Vega said.

“You can’t go behind the council and try to sell privatisation. How can you even think that you could still sit there? In any public company, if you are a member or board member, never mind actually the chairman of the board, and you are behind the scenes selling the shares? A resignation on the next day is automatic.

“I am not sure where the council are with this, but that should be immediate resignation. From my point of view, if he had any balls and had some moral and ethical understanding what the damage is here, he should come out and put his hands up and say sorry.

“Mistakes? I don't believe in mistakes at this level. This was not a mistake. This was on purpose. Mistakes you can do potentially in another way, but we are talking here one of the biggest selloffs ever in world football and one of the biggest assets of world football. That is something where you can't make mistakes. It is impossible. I don't accept mistakes in this case.

“This is completely delusional mismanagement how that has been handled and by default the resignation should be done. But it is not going to happen, because we know the man we are dealing with here.”

For Vega’s campaign to succeed, he has many hurdles yet to clear. To that end, he confirmed that he has already reached out to the Scottish FA about supporting his candidacy as he attempts to secure the five nominations required to make the ballot.

“First of all, I have a great relationship with Scottish football anyway because I played there. I have a natural affinity with it through Celtic. It's a natural kind of forum to talk.

“I am going to speak to the SFA and I hope the Scots and SFA now also understand from where I'm coming from in their mind as well and support. I think I can help them quite a lot, and not from an intimidation way.

“Scottish football has an important part within FIFA because it's one of the foundations of the invention of football with England, as well the oldest rules of the game. So, that's very important and I'm very proud that I actually played in that country as well.

“For me, it would be absolutely enormous pleasure that they support me in going forward.”

In positioning himself almost as the anti-Infantino, it may be helpful to also know a little about the man we are dealing with on the other side of this coin outside of his brief love affair with Celtic.

After retiring from the professional game, the former Tottenham Hotspur, Watford and Switzerland defender carved out a career in international finance, and has since advised football clubs on finance, commercial growth, strategy and football development.

He first explored running for the FIFA presidency in 2015 and made subsequent efforts in 2019 and 2022, without ultimately making the ballot. His last campaign ended under what he describes as ‘difficult and deeply questionable circumstances’.

Undeterred, Vega, alongside his wife Claudia, who has an extensive background at the highest level of the professional game herself in technical and operational roles, has produced an 11-page document outlining why the time is now right for him to pick up that fight once more.

At its heart is a sense that football – the fans, the players and the many other stakeholders – are desperate for change; that there is a hunger for a different type of leadership, where supporters are the priority, and not simply treated as customers.

While not naïve to the political machinations of an organisation on the scale of FIFA, he is also an unashamed romantic about the power of football to inspire, and it is the disillusionment of fans and their potential disconnection from the game that is his greatest fear.

“I played the game at the highest level, I served the game at the highest level. I have been giving to the game everything possible for where I am today. If I look back now, without the game, I couldn't talk now today with you regarding the game going forward or potentially lead the future of that game.

“I think it was important to put such a document together because people need to understand where this all comes from. It is coming with an enormous experience behind the scenes. People will see these guys are not coming in, and even though we are not inside the system, we understand the system extremely well. It was a lot of work from my wife as well because she understands the industry.

“If you think of the ticket prices for the World Cup as an example, I find this insane. The people's game, the game I played since I was a little boy, the one at an early stage I had access to for a small amount of money, which inspired me to go into football.

“When you start to minimise or even limit access because you start to be an elite game just to squeeze another pound, then you’re doing something completely wrong. You are not creating legacy or growth, inspiring the next generation.

“Let’s say you are a father, you’ve got two kids, they love football. Then there is a huge World Cup coming up, and you’re spending five to ten grand because you’re going to do as a father everything to show your kids the best of the best, which is the World Cup. But a father has to take out a mortgage or is taking credit and paying off for the next three to five years? That is not fair.

“And then you are also limiting because not everybody can afford this. Most football fans are not coming from a champagne background. They are the soul and I love them because they have really the proper passion.

“When I was at Celtic that was one of the best periods of time and one of the best set of fans on the planet because they’re coming with passion; they do everything for that club.

“We need to make sure that these fans leave also a legacy for their kids and the kids of their kids. But if you stop them because it is too expensive, you’re completely missing the whole inspiration of the young generation to play football in the future.

“You can see the society is ready for change. That's what we want. The fans have been neglected massively. They have been used as a customer. They are not customers; they are loyal fans and they have to be respected.”

As Vega wryly notes, though, it isn’t always a commitment towards the betterment of football or advancing the interests of the rank-and-file fan that gains the support of member federations, but the distribution of cold, hard cash.

He finds it staggering that FIFA now holds a reserve of $6bn and believes the distribution of FIFA funds has effectively become a form of political currency. His proposal is that more than 90 per cent of money coming into FIFA should be distributed to its member associations rather than accumulating within the organisation.

“There is no doubt that this way of being afraid to speak out, because they have certain commitments already done, shouldn't really happen,” he said.

“At the end of the day, it is the interest of the game and not the interest of a personal achievement. If you start to doubt the integrity or your moral or ethical side, then you have to ask yourself in the mirror: do I really do something to serve this game, or am I part of all of them in this gang?

“I think it is important now that people understand that can’t be happening anymore. They are still afraid because we went through it with lots of other federations. This is why it makes me angry because you don't develop the football side; it is more political currency that comes into place.

“Once the political currency is involved, then it is a wrong path. We are not going in the right direction.

“Let me just be very clear. Any money that comes into FIFA belongs to the associations. There are certain accountabilities in terms of how you manage that money. But in my view, 90 percent plus of that money has to be distributed immediately to all these associations with certain check balances and accountability.

“Holding back that kind of level of reserves when I see some of the smaller African or Asian federations, where they are struggling to have under 15s, under 16s traveling to another friendly game because the costs are so high. This is what you guys potentially need to explain as well in the UK or in Europe. If you withhold the money for these people, you’re stopping the development.

“That is one thing that definitely needs to be changed and not to be using it as a currency. At the moment, I feel it. You do something for me and I’m going to distribute that money. Like now, he [Infantino] says, 'I am going to send you all 25 million dollars.' But he isn't sending anything; that is their money. You are not doing anything else better than giving back what is their money.

“Why is that not yet distributed and why was this not mentioned at the Vancouver Congress? That's the question - why is that mentioned only now when the topic [of the sell-off] comes into it. Because of intimidation, and the fear of standing up for something that is right.

“We need to come back to a democratic way of standing up. Either you can agree or disagree, that's the way that democracy works. But you can't have an autocratic way of dealing with this. It is impossible.”

It is safe to say, then, that under Vega’s watch, spectacles such as the awarding of the FIFA Peace Prize to Donald Trump, or a national leader personally lobbying the FIFA president over a disciplinary case involving his country's team, would be a thing of the past.

Trump, infamously, personally asked Infantino to review USA striker Folarin Balogun's red card during the World Cup before the implementation of the forward's automatic one-match suspension was controversially suspended.

“Well, this is what happens when you’re cosying up with these people,” he said.

“I understand that it is the host nation. I have been in the game for a long time as well and I understand the politics, but you don't cosy up. You are friendly, you respect them, but there is a distance from the politics.

“Yes, I respect the host, I respect they put money into the hosting it. That's where it stays. Why do we need to have a peace award? I wouldn’t do that at all. That's not my responsibility, it is not my mandate. My mandate is to make sure the game is run properly with integrity and the distribution of the money goes to the right associations, and to make sure the grassroots has the right infrastructure to grow. That's my mandate.

“Giving a peace award? Not my mandate. That's for the Nobel Prize committee. They are an institution that has been doing this for years; they are responsible, they are respectable within the industry. That's not a matter of FIFA.

“It is the same thing when people are calling you up, 'Listen, I’d rather they do this.' That's the very question; I would say it is not my duty. I'm not answering them with, ‘I'm going to look into it’, like a partner in crime.

“I understand the motion from the American point of view. They want to make sure their country gets the best.

“But as a FIFA president, you have to tell them, 'Listen, it is not my responsibility. I'm sorry, I can't help you.' And you leave it there. That's it. That should have been a five-second discussion.”

Vega remains remarkably clued up about goings-on in Scottish football, particularly, and perhaps predictably, the fortunes of his former manager Martin O'Neill, who he was pleasantly surprised to see pulling on the old tracksuit once more.

“He’s still got it. I sent him a message when he took over again. We have always had a good rapport.

“Everyone knows he’s a legend. He’s done an unbelievable job for that club. In the twenty-first century, I would say he’s the best manager Celtic had in terms of achievements and from where he took Celtic. After nine years of Rangers’ domination, he came and won the treble straight away. It’s remarkable.

“This is what sport is all about, the legacy you leave behind, and not the money. Legacy is more important to be remembered by.

"At that age, I think it’s refreshing for someone to take over that job. Once you have that management style, it never goes away. You just have to adapt to the new football world and how the youngster’s mentality is different to 20 or 30 years ago. That’s the only difference between coaches from twenty years ago and now, it’s adapting to the new world we are living in.

“Sometimes it’s good to have that conservative past as I still think that works best with a balance."

By reframing the role of FIFA president as one of service to the game rather than the accumulation of personal power or wealth, Vega hopes he will win over many beyond these borders too, who have become disillusioned with the way the game is presently run.

“Not the individual person. That's very important to understand and that's the way I want to have it. We need to go back to a democratic way of decisions and accountability of the organisation.

“I think the president is there to make sure he is an ambassador, make sure that it is running properly. It doesn't mean he is the dictator or the autocratic way of dealing in it. He has to have consensus within all the member associations, the council. Respect, not like the disrespect now and the betrayal.

“That [the attempted sell-off] was betrayal. I don't know how these guys can still shake the hands of such a person who would betray them like that. Because that's really a betrayal from my point of view.

“So, it's important to make it not autocratic anymore, make it democratic. Put a formula in place and certain accountability and check balances. Certain power to be limited to a certain level because you still need to have certain authority to do it. That's what it is. But in a democratic way.

“It is one of the first things we need to look to in the first 100 days. I am inviting all the stakeholders to the table to put the consensus together how they view things, how we view things, how we’re going to change that. So, everybody is responsible too because in the end, the organisation belongs to the associations. That has to be very clear. It belongs to the association, not to me. Absolutely.

“It's very important that the guy who is running FIFA now knows it.”

#2123. Arsenal transfer news: Arteta considers January moves for two highly rated attackers

Lucie Turner writes about Arsenal for Read Arsenal and Newcastle United for Read Newcastle. A long-time Arsenal supporter, she also follows rugby, snooker, darts and Formula 1. Her work covers match previews and reports, breaking news, player profiles and opinion pieces.

Arsenal could have two ambitious attacking signings in mind for January. Eli Junior Kroupi and Kenan Yildiz are reportedly attracting Mikel Arteta’s attention.

Both players have emerged as potential targets as the Gunners consider further changes to their forward line. However, Arsenal may face a battle to prise either youngster away from their current club.

Kroupi is already familiar with the Premier League after making an impressive start to life at Bournemouth. The forward arrived from Lorient and scored 13 goals in 35 appearances across all competitions during his first season in England.

His development was halted by a broken foot in pre-season which required an operation to fix and kept him out of action for around three months.

The 20-year-old has now returned to the Bournemouth squad and has played as a substitute in their last three games. He will be hoping to get more game time as he gets back into full fitness.

Arsenal were linked with a move for Kroupi in the summer. His return to action has reignited interest in the player. A number of other European clubs also understood to be watching his situation.

Kroupi has attracted interest from a variety of top clubs including Manchester United, Tottenham, Bayern Munich and Paris Saint-Germain.

The French Under-21 international has impressed with his goal scoring in his first campaign in England. At only 20 years old, he has plenty of development to do under an experienced manager like Arteta.

However, Bournemouth are said to be reluctant to let Kroupi leave in January. Selling a bright young forward would leave Marco Rose with a tough decision to make.

It could get even more complicated if Alex Scott is also being seriously pursued. Chelsea and Liverpool are both linked with the midfielder.

Arsenal may therefore struggle to convince the Cherries to part with Kroupi during the winter window.

Another exciting option for Arsenal comes in the form of Juventus winger Yildiz. The winger has been linked with a move to North London for a number of transfer windows. He has once again been in the news recently at the end of the summer.

However, the Turkish international has struggled to get going on this campaign. He has only featured once in Serie A and has failed to feature since he suffered an injury.

Juventus have previously shown little interest in letting him leave. Head coach Luciano Spalletti firmly dismissed the prospect during the summer. Indicating that even an offer worth €100 million would not change the club’s position.

However, things could change next season. There are reports from Italy that Juventus may have to sell their star player if they fail to make the Champions League.

The Turin-based outfit are currently sitting in seventh spot in Serie A after just 10 games and are six points away from the qualifying spots.

This means that if they don’t make it into Europe, then it could put more pressure on Arsenal’s pursuit of Yildiz. It could create more pressure on them to raise funds.

Arsenal had a very busy summer with the addition of Bruno Guimaraes, Christos Tzolis, Ezri Konsa, Piero Hincapie and Illan Meslier.

But they also missed out on a few big names, such as Vinicius Jr and Julian Alvarez.

Despite this, there is still interest in Kroupi and Yildiz, suggesting that Arteta is still looking to strengthen his attacking options. Both players would bring quality and potential to the squad; however, there are some issues with both.

There are many unknowns surrounding this situation. Bournemouth aren’t keen to sell Kroupi and Juventus aren’t in a great financial position.

This doesn’t mean that either player will definitely be moving to the Emirates but much will depend on what happens at their clubs in the coming months.

#2122. Chelsea co-sporting director Laurence Stewart to leave in leadership reshuffle

Gary Gowers is a sports journalist and the editor of Read Everton, where he leads daily coverage of Everton news, transfers and analysis. He also writes about Manchester City for Read Man City and motorsport for Read Motorsport. A former accountant, he became a freelance writer in 2011 and has written for BBC Sport and Metro.

Laurence Stewart is set to leave Chelsea by mutual consent after almost four years at Stamford Bridge, with the club opting to redistribute his responsibilities rather than appoint a direct replacement.

The departure is understood to be amicable and won’t take immediate effect. Stewart could continue attending matches before formally leaving, following discussions with co-owner Behdad Eghbali over the club’s evolving sporting structure.

The story was first reported by Matt Law of The Telegraph and Nizaar Kinsella of BBC Sport, with Ben Jacobs subsequently providing further details about how Chelsea intend to operate.

Stewart arrived from Monaco in October 2022 as technical director before becoming co-sporting director alongside Paul Winstanley in February 2023.

Chelsea’s day-to-day sporting leadership will now be shared between Winstanley, Sam Jewell and Joe Shields, each with defined responsibilities.

Winstanley will continue overseeing transfer negotiations, player contracts and several departments at Cobham, including medical and performance operations. Shields’ work centres on the academy and player pathways, while Jewell has wider responsibilities across the BlueCo operation, including Strasbourg.

According to Jacobs, discussions about restructuring began in April and accelerated following the appointment of Xabi Alonso as head coach.

Stewart had overseen investment in Chelsea’s data analysis operation, although Jewell and Shields have increasingly taken responsibility for using that information in recruitment and talent identification.

Dave Fallows will remain involved in an advisory capacity, rather than taking on a fixed day-to-day portfolio.

The changes are being described as a natural evolution of Chelsea’s sporting model. There is no suggestion that Stewart’s departure followed a breakdown in relations, and the club are not currently planning to recruit a replacement.

#2121. PM: Milan enter top 10 for European shirt sponsor deals with Adidas – the figures

With this morning’s news about AC Milan’s deal with Adidas, they have entered the top 10 most expensive deals in Europe.

Earlier today, it was reported that Milan are set to sign a deal worth €500 million with Adidas, to become their new technical sponsors for the next 10 seasons. A monumental deal with a significant commercial impact, it’s a major step forward for the club.

Read SempreMilan ad-free and get access to exclusive news. Click here for a free trial!

In recent days, Gerry Cardinale also signed a deal with Generali for the stadium sponsorship, so it’s been a big week for the American.

As PianetaMilan write, the Rossoneri are now in the European top 10 when it comes to sponsorship deals. However, there is still a lot to overcome in terms of finances. For example, Real Madrid’s sponsorship deal is worth almost €100m more, at €120m per season.

Manchester City (ironically, €115m per season), Barcelona (€108m per season), Manchester United (€105m per season) and PSG (€100m per season) are still very far away. Then, Arsenal boast €85m per season, whilst Liverpool and Chelsea earn €70m per season.

Finally, you come to the Diavolo, who sit in 10th for the most earned from their technical sponsor. What is interesting, though, is that they are Adidas’ fifth-biggest club in Europe, behind Real Madrid, Manchester United, Arsenal and Liverpool.

#2120. Arsenal U19s face tough Slavia Prague test in UEFA Youth League

Lucie Turner writes about Arsenal for Read Arsenal and Newcastle United for Read Newcastle. A long-time Arsenal supporter, she also follows rugby, snooker, darts and Formula 1. Her work covers match previews and reports, breaking news, player profiles and opinion pieces.

Arsenal’s under-19s face a significant challenge when they travel to Czechia to take on Slavia Prague in the UEFA Youth League on Wednesday.

The young Gunners have picked up four points from their first three games and will be hoping to build on that in the league phase. Two wins would definitely help their chances of getting into the knockout stages.

They kicked off their campaign with a win against Napoli and put on a great show in a 3-3 draw against Bayern Munich last month.

Those results have left them 18th in the standings, 16 places below their next opponents. With the top 22 teams progressing to the knockout stages, another positive result could prove important for their qualification hopes.

However, Slavia Prague has made a much stronger start and will provide a difficult examination for David Horseman’s side.

Slavia have won all three of their first games in the UEFA Youth League and are sitting in second place. Just behind Real Madrid on goal difference.

They began well with a 2-1 win against French side Lens. They then went on to beat Sabah 3-0 and then put in another strong display against Fenerbahce.

Despite falling behind in Prague, the players fought back to win the game 3-1. The three wins means Slavia are already past the number of points they scored last season in the league stage.

They also advanced to the knockout stages last time, finishing 20th before they faced Benfica.

Mubarak Suleiman put the team ahead twice, but they were pegged back by two goals in the last 30 minutes as their European run came to an end.

This season they have looked like a much stronger team. With a perfect record, they will be one of the hardest teams that Arsenal will face.

The meeting on Wednesday provides an opportunity for Arsenal to get revenge for their heavy defeat in Prague last season.

The two sides met during the 2025/26 league campaign, with Slavia winning 5-1 at home.

Arsenal were three goals down at the break after an own goal plus goals from Bartosz Szywala and Jakub Kolisek.

O’Neill pulled one back after the break, but the home side had the final word as Suleiman and Matej Kotrba scored in stoppage time.

This loss shows how tough it will be for Horseman’s side. Slavia have already proven their attacking prowess against Arsenal and are playing even better in this campaign.

The young Gunners will have to play much more disciplined if they are to leave Prague with three points.

Arsenal fans can watch the UEFA Youth League clash on Wednesday.

It will be streamed via the Arsenal app and Arsenal.com, with the match taking place at the Sports Centre Radotin.

Updates will also be posted here and on the club’s Academy X page.

A full match report, highlights and post-match interviews with Horseman and one of the young players will be published on Arsenal’s official channels.

With them still in contention to qualify for the knockout stages, Arsenal will be looking to get off to a good start. Slavia’s great early start means they will have to play some of their best football of the tournament.

#2119. Chelsea v Bournemouth: Kick-off time, TV details and how to follow

Gary Gowers is a sports journalist and the editor of Read Everton, where he leads daily coverage of Everton news, transfers and analysis. He also writes about Manchester City for Read Man City and motorsport for Read Motorsport. A former accountant, he became a freelance writer in 2011 and has written for BBC Sport and Metro.

There will be no live UK television coverage of Chelsea’s return to Premier League action against Bournemouth on Saturday afternoon.

Xabi Alonso’s side host the Cherries at Stamford Bridge in a traditional 3pm kick-off, with the fixture falling within the Saturday afternoon broadcasting blackout.

That means supporters unable to attend will need to follow the match through other channels, rather than watching it live on Sky Sports or TNT Sports.

Although the game will not be televised live in the UK, supporters can follow developments through the official Chelsea website and the Premier League website.

The Saturday 3pm blackout prevents live domestic television broadcasts during the restricted period, although overseas coverage may be available through broadcasters holding the relevant rights.

Highlights will be available after the match through the usual broadcast and digital channels, subject to their respective release schedules.

Chelsea will then turn their attention to a trip to Everton on Saturday, 17 October, with that fixture scheduled for a 12.30pm kick-off at Hill Dickinson Stadium.

For Alonso, the Bournemouth match provides the immediate opportunity to resume Chelsea’s domestic campaign following the international break.

#2118. Arsenal handed major William Saliba injury boost ahead of Manchester City showdown

Lucie Turner writes about Arsenal for Read Arsenal and Newcastle United for Read Newcastle. A long-time Arsenal supporter, she also follows rugby, snooker, darts and Formula 1. Her work covers match previews and reports, breaking news, player profiles and opinion pieces.

Arsenal have received a significant boost with William Saliba returning to first-team training. He’s spent almost four months out with a back injury.

The French international was seen returning to training on the pitch. Sparking hope that he could be back in action soon for Mikel Arteta.

Saliba has been sidelined since he suffered the injury in France’s 2-0 semi-final defeat to Spain. The defender was forced off after only 30 minutes after playing the full 90 in his previous five starts at the tournament.

The player was confirmed by Arsenal to be sidelined for a lengthy period of time in July. He did not require surgery, meaning he can begin working back into action.

His latest progress comes at an important point in Arsenal’s season, with several major fixtures approaching.

The 25-year-old has travelled to the Czech Republic with Arsenal’s squad to face Slavia Prague in the Champions League.

Arteta confirmed that Saliba had played for 45 minutes in a behind-closed-moors game last week as part of his rehab.

He then returned to full first team training on Tuesday, providing another defensive option for the club.

Arteta spoke ahead of the game against Slavia Prague. He said that Saliba is happy with his recovery and is at a point where he will be used if needed.

The Arsenal manager has not confirmed if the Frenchman will be involved tonight.

Saliba has not played in a competitive game since losing to Spain in the World Cup semi-final for France. So it will be his first involvement in a competitive game since the summer.

Arsenal have coped well with Saliba’s absence.

Gabriel initially partnered Cristhian Mosquera at centre-back before the Spaniard suffered an ankle injury. This allowed Ezri Konsa to come into the starting line-up.

Ezri Konsa settled into a partnership with the Brazilian. He joined from Aston Villa for £51 million in the summer.

The partnership has helped the Gunners to have the best defensive record in the Premier League. Having only conceded 5 goals in their first 9 games.

Now that Saliba is available, Arteta has more options and flexibility with his defenders.

The manager has had past success with Saliba and Gabriel as his first-choice central defensive pairing. Their understanding has played a big part in Arsenal’s defensive displays.

However, Konsa has provided Arteta with another selection dilemma to consider when Saliba is back to full fitness.

Arsenal have a busy schedule ahead after today’s Champions League game at Slavia Prague.

On Saturday they play Hull City, followed by games against Manchester City, Real Madrid and Tottenham after the international break in November.

If Saliba can come back in, it will give Arteta more defensive options to play with, but they won’t want to rush him back.

The defender played 50 times last season, helping Arsenal win the Premier League and reach the Champions League final. He also ranked 29th in the men’s Ballon d’Or in October.

His return to training is a major step in his road to full fitness, but it remains to be seen if he will play against Slavia Prague tonight.

#2117. Chelsea’s interest in Alex Scott remains despite Bournemouth transfer stance

Gary Gowers is a sports journalist and the editor of Read Everton, where he leads daily coverage of Everton news, transfers and analysis. He also writes about Manchester City for Read Man City and motorsport for Read Motorsport. A former accountant, he became a freelance writer in 2011 and has written for BBC Sport and Metro.

Bournemouth’s determination to keep Alex Scott has done little to discourage Chelsea, who remain among the leading contenders to sign the England midfielder.

The Blues had a £64m bid rejected during the summer, but their interest has continued into the new season. According to Matt Law of The Telegraph, Chelsea are at the front of the queue of clubs monitoring the 23-year-old.

Bournemouth, however, have no intention of allowing Scott to leave in January. talkSPORT reported this week that the south coast club remain determined to retain him, despite his reluctance to sign a contract extension.

Scott’s current deal runs until 2028, leaving Bournemouth with time to negotiate, although their position could become more complicated if no agreement is reached before next summer.

The midfielder’s immediate priority is recovering from a thigh injury sustained while representing England. He impressed during the 7-0 Nations League victory over Croatia but was withdrawn after 70 minutes. Subsequent examinations revealed a quadriceps problem, with BBC Sport reporting that he could miss six to eight weeks.

His absence is a setback for Bournemouth, who have relied heavily on the former Bristol City midfielder this season. It also introduces another consideration for clubs monitoring his progress ahead of the January window.

Chelsea are not alone in their admiration. Arsenal and Liverpool have also been linked with Scott, whose performances have attracted attention beyond the Premier League.

Suggestions circulating on social media that Bournemouth have already agreed to let him leave in January remain unsubstantiated. There has been no reliable confirmation of such an arrangement, and the club’s reported position is firmly against a winter sale.

For Chelsea, the more realistic opportunity may come next summer, particularly if Scott continues to resist signing a new contract.

#2116. One thing Italy boss Roberto Mancini did recently shows exactly what he thinks of Giovanni Leoni

Giovanni Leoni appears to be firmly in the mind of Italy head coach Roberto Mancini, judging by one recent report from his homeland.

The Liverpool defender has been out injured for more than a year after rupturing his ACL on his debut for the Reds against Southampton in September 2025, with the 19-year-old since undergoing a lengthy road to recovery.

Despite some hopes that he’d resume team training during the most recent international window a month ago, Andoni Iraola has since indicated that such a step will hopefully be taken during the upcoming round of national team fixtures in mid-November.

Even though Leoni hasn’t played a match of any description for more than 13 months, it seems that he hasn’t been forgotten by Mancini.

According to Gazzetta dello Sport, the Italy head coach travelled to England in the hope of seeing the Liverpool defender (among several other candidates for Azzurri selection) on the pitch, but the teenager hadn’t recovered in time for the 61-year-old’s visit.

The report stated that the Reds’ number 15 ‘may return after the international break’, although that didn’t specify whether it’d be in the form of team training or clearance to finally resume playing.

Want more Empire of the Kop coverage? Add us as a preferred source on Google to your favourites list for news you can trust

Even with Leoni sidelined for so long, that Mancini reportedly wanted to see him in action on his vist to England attests to how highly the Italy boss regards the Liverpool defender.

The heartbreaking ACL injury overshadowed just how well the centre-back played on his Reds debut against Southampton before the cruellest intervention of fate, and the ex-Manchester City manager clearly has plenty of faith in the 19-year-old’s abilities.

The day that the young Italian is finally back in action will be one to cherish, although it’ll naturally take him time to get back into the rhythm of playing after being ruled out for 13 months and counting.

He could also have a difficult task on his hands trying to break up the uncompromising centre-back partnership of Virgil van Dijk and Jeremy Jacquet, with the latter particularly excelling since coming to Liverpool from Rennes in the summer.

Right now, though, simply getting back on the pitch will represent a seminal moment for Leoni, who should be given a tremendous boost by knowing that Mancini has him in his plans for the Italy squad.

#2115. Slavia Prague 1-2 Arsenal: Madueke and Gyokeres fire Gunners to Champions League victory

Lucie Turner writes about Arsenal for Read Arsenal and Newcastle United for Read Newcastle. A long-time Arsenal supporter, she also follows rugby, snooker, darts and Formula 1. Her work covers match previews and reports, breaking news, player profiles and opinion pieces.

Arsenal moved up to third in the Champions League table after beating Slavia Prague 2-1 at the Fortuna Arena.

Noni Madueke opened the scoring after nine minutes, finishing calmly after Viktor Gyokeres sent him through on goal.

The Swedish striker then doubled Arsenal’s advantage with an excellent finish in the 38th minute. However, a much stronger second-half performance from Slavia made for an uncomfortable finish.

Wiktor Nowak scored a late goal to put pressure on the Gunners. They held on to take all three points.

Mikel Arteta made six changes from the Liverpool win on Sunday. William Saliba returns to the squad after his back injury.

The defender was left on the bench, with Ezri Konsa playing alongside Gabriel Magalhães in central defence.

Arsenal started brightly and took control of the game when they won the long ball forward. Gyokeres beat his man and found Madueke with a clever through ball.

The winger used his pace to get into the penalty area before sliding his finish through goalkeeper Nazar Domchak’s legs.

Arsenal continued to create chances, with Mikel Merino going close on several occasions. Domchak also produced an important save to deny the midfielder from close range.

Merino eventually helped Arsenal extend their lead. After winning possession in the Slavia half, Declan Rice found Gyokeres, who exchanged passes with the Spaniard before curling a superb finish into the far corner.

The visitors could have added a third before the break. Gabriel’s header from a corner was cleared off the line.

Arsenal appeared to be in control at half-time.

But Slavia upped the tempo and started to create more space, forcing Arsenal into a defensive stance. Early on, Oskar Kubiak fired a long-range shot, forcing David Raya to push it away.

Arteta got increasingly frustrated as Arsenal struggled to hold on to the ball and dominate the midfield.

They tried to keep pressing, but Arsenal struggled to create much at the other end. Madueke did get into a good position to shoot from an angle but didn’t have enough power to cause any problems for Domchak.

Slavia were denied a goal when Gabriel headed the ball away from the goal line. Raya made a brilliant stop to keep the shot out while the penalty area was crowded.

The Arsenal keeper made another stunning save as Kubiak powered a header from a cross. Raya deflected the ball onto the post to keep his team ahead by two goals.

Slavia scored their breakthrough goal in the 90th minute after David Jurasek played a low cross from the left. Nowak was able to run into the box to score past Raya.

It created a nervy few minutes of added time as Arsenal had to defend their lead.

Slavia could find no way back into the game as Stepan Chaloupek headed wide and Mikulas Konecny threatened in the dying moments, but his effort lacked confidence as Raya made light work of saving.

Arsenal secured their third win in their last four Champions League games to move to 10 points and into third place in the group stage.

The Gunners have now gone unbeaten in their last ten Champions League away games to set a new club record.

However, Arteta will be looking for a much better display from his side when they resume action in the Premier League against Hull City on Saturday.

#2114. Chelsea make early move for Palmeiras teenager Eduardo Conceicao

Gary Gowers is a sports journalist and the editor of Read Everton, where he leads daily coverage of Everton news, transfers and analysis. He also writes about Manchester City for Read Man City and motorsport for Read Motorsport. A former accountant, he became a freelance writer in 2011 and has written for BBC Sport and Metro.

Chelsea have reportedly made contact with Palmeiras as they attempt to gain an early advantage in the race to sign Brazilian teenager Eduardo Conceicao.

The 16-year-old forward has attracted attention from several leading European clubs, but Chelsea are understood to be among the first to establish direct contact with Palmeiras over a potential future transfer.

According to Mark Brus of CaughtOffside, the London club have held a series of discussions as they seek to position themselves ahead of their rivals.

Palmeiras are reportedly preparing for substantial offers, with some figures at the Brazilian club discussing a valuation as high as €100m (£85m).

That figure has not been established as a firm asking price, however, and Brus reports that industry sources expect the eventual cost to be lower. Chelsea’s previous investment of approximately £51m in Estevao is regarded as a possible benchmark.

Manchester City’s ownership group have also made contact regarding Conceicao, while Arsenal, Manchester United, Barcelona, Real Madrid, Bayern Munich and Paris Saint-Germain are among the clubs reported to have watched him.

Chelsea’s existing relationship with Palmeiras could help their efforts, although the level of competition means there is no guarantee that their early interest will lead to a deal.

The player’s age is another consideration. Conceicao cannot complete an international transfer to England until he turns 18, leaving interested clubs facing a potentially lengthy wait before he can join their squads.

Chelsea have already demonstrated their willingness to agree deals for young players well before they are eligible to arrive, with Estevao providing a particularly relevant example.

No formal bid or agreement has been reported for Conceicao. Nevertheless, the latest claims suggest Chelsea are prepared to explore another long-term investment in Brazilian talent rather than wait until the player becomes available for an immediate move.

#2113. Arsenal injury latest: Konsa concern, Saliba return and Saka update

Lucie Turner writes about Arsenal for Read Arsenal and Newcastle United for Read Newcastle. A long-time Arsenal supporter, she also follows rugby, snooker, darts and Formula 1. Her work covers match previews and reports, breaking news, player profiles and opinion pieces.

Arsenal will hope Ezri Konsa recovers in time for Saturday’s 3pm Premier League clash with Hull City after he came off during their Champions League win over Slavia Prague.The return of William Saliba to the matchday squad is a great boost for Mikel Arteta ahead of the game, but Bukayo Saka remains out as he continues to recover from his quad injury.

Here is all the latest Arsenal injury news and when the three players are expected to return.

Konsa started Arsenal’s 2-1 victory over Slavia Prague on Wednesday but came off in the second half after experiencing discomfort.

He appeared to limp during the first half, so Arsenal will monitor his fitness closely ahead of Saturday’s match.

Arteta said the change was not a tactical one, as Konsa had been playing a lot of games recently and had some problems during the match.

The Arsenal boss is likely to give an update ahead of Saturday’s game depending on how he recovers in the coming days.

Konsa’s absence will leave Arsenal without as many reliable centre backs. However, Gabriel will play a key role in the backline, and Arteta has other options in Cristhian Mosquera and Piero Hincapie.

Potential return date: November 7 vs Hull City (3pm kick-off).

A positive from the Gunners’ trip to Prague was the inclusion of Saliba in the squad.

The defender has not played this season after picking up a long-term back injury. The issue got worse during his time with France at the World Cup finals, but he managed to avoid surgery.

Saliba has steadily built up his workload and played for 45 minutes in a behind-closed-moors game. He returned to full training on Tuesday and travelled with the squad to take on Slavia Prague.

Although he did not come off the bench, his inclusion shows he is getting close to being back in contention.

Arteta said that Saliba was feeling confident, but it will depend on the needs of the squad.

It gives Arsenal an extra experienced centre-back, and if Konsa is not available against Hull, then he could make an appearance on the pitch.

Potential return date: November 7 vs Hull City (3pm kick-off).

Saka is currently the main attacking concern for Arsenal after he has been sidelined with a quad injury for the last four games.

The winger suffered the injury approximately 10 minutes into the Arsenal Champions League clash against Bayern Munich last month.

The original fears were that Saka would be out for up to eight weeks. Arsenal have not given any confirmation on how long it will take him to make a full recovery.

He will need to undergo another scan to get a better idea of the amount of swelling and fluid in the area. The medical staff will also want to see if the tendon is involved.

This means it is unlikely he plays against Hull. Arsenal are not expected to bring him back too quickly ahead of the international break.

Arteta will miss one of his most influential attackers. Hopefully the next scan will give a clearer indication of how long he is likely to be out for.

Expected return date: Unknown. Saka is not expected to face Hull City on November 7.

Arsenal host Hull City at the Emirates Stadium on Saturday, November 7th.

The game comes ahead of a tough sequence of games which sees Manchester City, Real Madrid and Tottenham on the horizon. Arteta will want as many defensive and attacking threats on hand.

#2112. GdS: Milan risk being without Chukwueze until the spring after successful surgery

As a result of his surgery, AC Milan may be without Samuel Chukwueze for a little longer than was originally hoped.

Earlier today, Milan confirmed that Chukwueze had undergone surgery on his left hip in London, successfully, and it begun the rehabilitation process. The Nigerian will now work to return as soon as possible, but it won’t be in the near future, unfortunately.

Read SempreMilan ad-free and get access to exclusive news. Click here for a free trial!

As Gazzetta dello Sport writes this afternoon, the positive news is that his surgery was successful. However, it comes with some bad news about his time on the sidelines. Indeed, he was expected to be out until early 2027, but that may now be changed to the spring.

The initial hope was a return to training in December, but that will obviously be pushed back now as a result. Considering how big the spring could be for the Rossoneri, having him back available will only be a good thing.

Nevertheless, it would be preferable if he were back way before then…

The Diavolo may now step up their pursuit of recalling Zachary Athekame in January, thanks to this news, given that he’ll lessen the pressure on Chukwueze’s return. However, that will be a difficult situation in its own right.

#2111. Chelsea news: Stewart departure, Scott interest and Bournemouth preparations

Gary Gowers is a sports journalist and the editor of Read Everton, where he leads daily coverage of Everton news, transfers and analysis. He also writes about Manchester City for Read Man City and motorsport for Read Motorsport. A former accountant, he became a freelance writer in 2011 and has written for BBC Sport and Metro.

Chelsea’s sporting department is facing another change following news of Laurence Stewart’s impending departure, while the club continue to be linked with potential additions to their squad.

Alex Scott and Palmeiras teenager Eduardo Conceicao have both featured in Thursday’s transfer coverage, with Xabi Alonso also preparing his players for Saturday’s Premier League meeting with Bournemouth.

Here is our 7pm round-up of the latest Chelsea news for Thursday, 8 October.

Laurence Stewart is set to leave Chelsea by mutual consent after almost four years at Stamford Bridge, with the club planning to redistribute his responsibilities rather than appoint a direct replacement.

His departure is understood to be amicable and will not take immediate effect. Stewart joined from Monaco in 2022, initially as technical director, before becoming co-sporting director alongside Paul Winstanley.

As detailed in our report on Stewart’s departure and Chelsea’s leadership reshuffle, Winstanley, Sam Jewell and Joe Shields are expected to take on expanded responsibilities across recruitment, player development and the wider BlueCo operation.

The changes follow discussions about the club’s sporting structure that reportedly began earlier this year. There is no confirmed suggestion that Stewart’s departure resulted from a disagreement with Alonso.

Chelsea’s reported interest in Bournemouth midfielder Alex Scott remains a talking point, despite the south coast club’s determination to retain the England international.

The Blues reportedly had a £64m offer rejected during the summer, with Matt Law of The Telegraph subsequently identifying Chelsea as leading contenders for the 23-year-old.

However, as examined in our latest report on Chelsea’s interest in Scott, Bournemouth are not expected to entertain a January sale.

Scott’s immediate concern is recovering from a thigh injury suffered while representing England. The midfielder could face several weeks on the sidelines, potentially complicating Bournemouth’s plans as well as any future discussions about his next move.

Chelsea have also reportedly made contact with Palmeiras over 16-year-old forward Eduardo Conceicao, who is attracting attention from several leading European clubs.

According to Mark Brus of CaughtOffside, Chelsea have held discussions with the Brazilian club as they explore the possibility of a future deal.

Our report on Chelsea’s interest in Eduardo Conceicao explains that a valuation as high as €100m has been suggested, although that figure is not a confirmed asking price or evidence of a formal bid.

Manchester City, Arsenal and Real Madrid are among the other clubs reportedly monitoring the teenager. Conceicao would be unable to complete a transfer to England until he turns 18, making any prospective move a longer-term consideration.

Alonso’s attention will now turn to Saturday’s Premier League meeting with Bournemouth at Stamford Bridge.

The match kicks off at 3pm and will not be televised live in the United Kingdom because of the Saturday afternoon broadcasting blackout.

Our Chelsea v Bournemouth kick-off and how-to-watch guide provides the full details, including how to follow the match and the available overseas broadcast information.

Scott’s injury is expected to deprive Bournemouth of an important midfield option, although Chelsea will have their own fitness decisions to make as players return from international duty.

After Saturday’s fixture, Chelsea travel to Hill Dickinson Stadium to face Everton on 17 October. Alonso will be looking for greater consistency as the domestic programme resumes.

#2110. Motherwell ace feeling 21 again as he embraces senior role

Motherwell striker Callum Hendry aims to be a key player on and off the park after getting back to fitness.

Hendry made his first appearance of the season in Saturday’s 1-0 win over St Mirren following knee surgery, having come back from a four-month lay-off in April last season.

Speaking ahead of Saturday’s trip to face Dundee United, the former St Johnstone player said: “I sorted out the double hernia and then playing and training consistently probably covered the injury that I had.

“At the start of summer I started running again and I had really bad, really angry tendonitis, so it just needed fixing. I got the operation and I feel good now and hopefully I can put it behind me.

“I’m very optimistic. The more minutes I get, the fitter I’ll get, the sharper I’ll get. And I’ve seen that the team’s creating a lot of chances.”

Hendry, Paul McGinn and Oscar Priestman all returned off the bench against Saints to give the squad a lift.

“I’ve actually felt like there’s a bit of a glow around training having us back, because it’s such a tight-knit group,” the 28-year-old said. “It was just nice for us all to come back for the same game, and win it.

“The culture hasn’t changed, which I think is important, because we created such a good culture last year and we’ve kept it somehow, which is quite rare for such an overhaul of players.

“There’s a few younger lads in there, different changing room really, but still got the same feel to it. The boys are still close.

“It’s important that you stick together. They have had to stick together through some tough results the last couple of months where they haven’t really deserved them.

“I’ve had a few boys speak to me and ask me for advice, more this time than the last few seasons.

“I still feel 21, but my body tells me different sometimes. But it’s nice to be that guy because I always leaned on the older pros when I was younger.”

#2109. Arsenal vs Hull City preview: Gunners face draw specialists at the Emirates

Lucie Turner writes about Arsenal for Read Arsenal and Newcastle United for Read Newcastle. A long-time Arsenal supporter, she also follows rugby, snooker, darts and Formula 1. Her work covers match previews and reports, breaking news, player profiles and opinion pieces.

Arsenal have the opportunity to strengthen their position in the Premier League table when they welcome Hull City to the Emirates Stadium on Saturday.

Mikel Arteta’s side secured a three-point lead after their 1-0 victory over Liverpool at Anfield and then followed it up with a 2-1 win in the Champions League against Slavia Prague on Wednesday.

The Gunners have won four games in a row but are looking to stretch their lead to six points ahead of Manchester City’s clash against Nottingham Forest later on Saturday. They are undefeated at home this season.

However, Hull will be looking to cause problems in North London.

Sergej Jakirovic’s side sit 11th after collecting 11 points from their opening nine league matches.

They have drawn five times, sharing the highest number of stalemates in the division. Three of their last four league fixtures have also ended level.

They drew 2-2 with Ipswich Town in their last game, but they needed to get back on track after falling behind. Recently they have failed to convert good performances into wins.

Despite Arsenal expecting to dominate possession, Hull’s 5-man defence may cause them problems.

The visitors have also had defensive issues recently, as they have failed to keep a clean sheet in their last seven games in all competitions. This may lead to chances being created for the Gunners attackers.

Arteta has to deal with his squad after the midweek trip to Prague. William Saliba is back in contention after recovering from his back injury but Cristhian Mosquera may stay in the squad if he is not rushed back in.

Ezri Konsa is a doubt after being withdrawn from Wednesday’s game with a knee problem. Bukayo Saka is also still sidelined as is Max Dowman.

Hull will be missing Matt Crooks who is suspended. Eliot Matazo, Matt Targett, Darko Gyabi, Ilyas Ansah and Charlie Hughes are also expected to miss out.

Raya; Timber, Mosquera, Gabriel, Calafiori; Guimarães, Rice; Madueke, Ødegaard, Tzolis; Havertz.

Tzolakis; Coyle, Ajayi, Egan, Mendy, Giles; Belloumi, Iroegbunam, Slater, Stroud; McBurnie.

Arsenal will face Hull City at the Emirates Stadium on Saturday, November 7. Kick off is at 3pm GMT.

Despite the fact that Hull has a tendency to draw, Arsenal remains a strong favorite to win this match. Arteta’s team will want to earn as many points as possible ahead of the international break.

#2108. Thomas Tuchel makes Noni Madueke decision as five Arsenal stars earn England call-ups

Lucie Turner writes about Arsenal for Read Arsenal and Newcastle United for Read Newcastle. A long-time Arsenal supporter, she also follows rugby, snooker, darts and Formula 1. Her work covers match previews and reports, breaking news, player profiles and opinion pieces.

Noni Madueke has missed another England opportunity, despite making an encouraging impression during his recent run in the Arsenal starting line-up.

Thomas Tuchel has named his 25-man squad for the Nations League fixtures against Croatia and Spain in November, but Arsenal winger Madueke misses out again after failing to make the cut following a missed international camp.

He has been left out despite making the most of Bukayo Saka’s absence to increase his involvement under Mikel Arteta.

Madueke scored in the recent Premier League win against Nottingham Forest and then again in Wednesday’s Champions League win against Slavia Prague.

He had a legitimate claim to be named in the squad, and Tuchel admitted that Madueke was very close to being included.

The England boss acknowledged that Madueke has benefitted from his extra games at Arsenal due to Saka’s injury.

But Tuchel chose to keep the same front three that he had during the last international camp. The German wants to reward those players who have earned their place in the team and not make changes every time they are playing well at club level.

This means Madueke will be left frustrated, having to wait until next time.

Madueke has played under Tuchel before, so it shows how much he thinks of him as a player. He featured in England’s 2026 World Cup campaign, so it is noticeable that he was left out this time.

Although he can’t control the battle for places, he will now be able to continue building his case. With Saka still injured, he will have the chance to play more regularly and solidify his place in Arteta’s plans.

However, Madueke is not the only player at Arsenal who has had positive news.

Declan Rice, Eberechi Eze, Ezri Konsa and Myles Lewis-Skelly have all earned or retained their spots in Tuchel’s squad. Ben White also comes back into the England squad, having missed the last camp.

It is another chance for White to re-establish himself on the world stage after his knee injury kept him out of the World Cup.

It also means Arsenal have five players in the squad after Saka was left out due to injury.

England play Croatia at Wembley on November 12 before heading to Spain on November 15. Madueke will be watching from home, knowing he was almost part of the squad.

Now he needs to build on his recent form and become a consistent performer for Arsenal. Whilst Tuchel has left the door open, Madueke needs to give the England boss another reason to change his mind.

#2107. ‘Hopefully…’ – Andoni Iraola hints at potential Ryan Gravenberch return date after injury blow

Andoni Iraola has provided an update on the injury which has ruled Ryan Gravenberch out of Liverpool’s Champions League fixture against Fenerbahce.

The Merseyside club confirmed on Tuesday morning that the midfielder hasn’t travelled with the rest of the squad for the game in Istanbul tomorrow due to a heel injury and will instead undergo recovery at the AXA Training Centre.

While that obviously comes as a body blow for the Reds for the match against Dirk Kuyt’s side, it appears that the 24-year-old is unlikely to be sidelined for long.

Iraola was prompted for an update on Gravenberch during his pre-match press conference in Istanbul this afternoon, and the Liverpool head coach is hopeful that the Dutchman will be cleared to resume playing quickly.

The Reds boss said (via Liverpool Echo): “It’s a day-to-day thing. It’s a heel injury. He’s had some discomfort. He was warming up against Arsenal and was not 100%. Hopefully he can be available for the weekend, but we cannot say for sure.”

Want more Empire of the Kop coverage? Add us as a preferred source on Google to your favourites list for news you can trust

If Iraola has left the door open for Gravenberch to potentially return against Crystal Palace next Sunday, it suggests that the midfielder’s injury is relatively minor.

The 24-year-old mightn’t make it back in time for the visit to Selhurst Park, but the two-week international window should hopefully enable him to be fully fit for the grudge match against Manchester United later this month.

The Dutchman was criticised for his performance against Chelsea in the Carabao Cup last week and in recent games has been disapponting by his standards, but he remains a vital player to Liverpool and one of the best midfielders in the Premier League when operating at his top level.

Even if he’s cleared to play on Sunday, Gravenberch seems unlikely to start given the form of Alexis Mac Allister and Trey Nyoni in the middle of the park, and he mightn’t be able to feature for a full 90 minutes if he’s touch-and-go for the weekend.

Fingers crossed that our number 38 will at least be available to Iraola when the Reds take on Palace with one eye on revenge for their last-gasp defeat in this fixture last season.
